### [1] [TSCA-Net: Temporal-Spatial Clique Attention for Interpretable Multimodal Pedestrian Trajectory Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11939)
*Md Mustafizur Rahman,Guangchao Yang,A F M Abdun Noor,Md Imam Ahasan,Md Mahfuzur Rahman,Md Ariful Islam*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TSCA-Net is a pedestrian trajectory prediction framework with three novel modules—Temporal-Spatial Clique Attention (time-aware goal-history interaction), Cross-Pedestrian Clique Potential (asymmetric social modeling), and Adaptive KAN Grid Refinement (dynamic decoder complexity)—achieving SOTA results on ETH/UCY (0.13/0.20m ADE/FDE) and SDD (6.95/10.43 pixels).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing goal-conditioned trajectory prediction models suffer from static displacement structures (equal weighting of all historical steps), standard graph attention mechanisms (lacking asymmetric social modeling), and fixed-capacity decoders (cannot adapt to local prediction complexity), failing to address multimodal uncertainty and variable motion dynamics in crowded scenes.

Method: TSCA-Net comprises three modules: (1) TSCA introduces learnable temporal gating into clique-based goal-history interaction for time-aware modulation; (2) CPCP models asymmetric pairwise agent relationships via a dynamic clique potential framework with a time-varying social graph; (3) AKGR dynamically adjusts B-spline grid resolution in a KAN-augmented LSTM decoder based on per-agent goal distribution entropy.

Result: State-of-the-art performance on ETH/UCY (average ADE 0.13m, FDE 0.20m) and Stanford Drone Dataset (ADE 6.95 pixels, FDE 10.43 pixels). Ablation studies confirm complementary contributions of all three modules.

Conclusion: TSCA-Net effectively addresses limitations of existing goal-conditioned trajectory prediction by introducing adaptive temporal weighting, asymmetric social interaction modeling, and dynamic decoder complexity, achieving SOTA results on two major benchmarks.

Abstract: Accurate pedestrian trajectory prediction in crowded environments remains challenging due to the multimodal uncertainty of human motion and the variable complexity of motion dynamics across different scene contexts. Existing goal-conditioned models rely on static displacement structures that assign equal weight to all historical time steps, standard graph attention mechanisms, and fixed-capacity motion decoders that cannot adapt to local prediction complexity. To address these limitations, we propose TSCA-Net, a trajectory prediction framework built upon three complementary modules. The Temporal-Spatial Clique Attention (TSCA) module introduces learnable temporal gating into clique-based goal-history interaction, enabling time-aware modulation of historical observations relative to each candidate goal. The Cross-Pedestrian Clique Potential (CPCP) module models asymmetric pairwise agent relationships through a dynamic clique potential framework with a time-varying social graph. The Adaptive KAN Grid Refinement (AKGR) mechanism dynamically adjusts the B-spline grid resolution of a Kolmogorov-Arnold Network-augmented LSTM decoder based on per-agent goal distribution entropy, balancing model expressiveness against overfitting across varying motion complexities. Extensive experiments on the ETH/UCY and Stanford Drone Dataset benchmarks demonstrate that TSCA-Net achieves state-of-the-art performance, with average ADE/FDE of 0.13/0.20 m on ETH/UCY and 6.95/10.43 pixels on SDD. Comprehensive ablation studies confirm the complementary contributions of all three proposed modules.

</details>


### [2] [GenDiff: A Dose and Anatomy Aware Diffusion Model with Structural Prior Refinement for Low-Dose CT Reconstruction and Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11941)
*Md Imam Ahasan,Guangchao Yang,A F M Abdun Noor,Kah Ong Michael Goh,S. M. Hasan Mahmud,Md Mahfuzur Rahman*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GenDiff is a diffusion-based framework for low-dose CT reconstruction that jointly models continuous radiation dose and anatomical information, integrating a Dose-Anatomy Encoder, cold diffusion backbone, physics-consistency update, and Structural Prior Refinement Module to achieve superior and robust reconstruction across diverse dose levels and anatomical regions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing deep learning LDCT reconstruction methods are optimized for fixed dose levels or specific anatomical regions, limiting their robustness and generalization in realistic clinical settings where dose and anatomy vary.

Method: GenDiff integrates a Dose-Anatomy Encoder (acquisition-aware embeddings), a dose- and anatomy-conditioned cold diffusion backbone (iterative refinement), a physics-consistency update (CT forward model fidelity), and a Structural Prior Refinement Module (anatomical preservation and artifact suppression).

Result: Outperforms state-of-the-art CNN and diffusion-based methods on multi-anatomy clinical datasets, unseen ultra-low-dose conditions, and out-of-distribution phantom/animal datasets, achieving superior reconstruction quality and robustness.

Conclusion: GenDiff is a promising solution for practical low-dose CT imaging, demonstrating strong generalization across different dose levels, anatomical regions, and acquisition domains.

Abstract: Computed tomography (CT) is a critical imaging modality for clinical diagnosis, but reducing radiation dose inevitably introduces severe noise and structured artifacts that degrade image quality. Existing deep learning-based low-dose CT (LDCT) reconstruction methods are typically optimized for fixed dose levels or specific anatomical regions, limiting their robustness and generalization in realistic clinical settings. We propose GenDiff, a generalizable diffusion-based framework for LDCT reconstruction that jointly models continuous radiation dose and anatomical information within a unified reconstruction network. The proposed framework integrates a Dose-Anatomy Encoder to learn acquisition-aware embeddings, a dose- and anatomy-conditioned cold diffusion backbone for iterative refinement, a physics-consistency update to enforce fidelity to the CT forward model, and a Structural Prior Refinement Module (SPRM) that preserves anatomical structures while suppressing dose-dependent artifacts. Extensive experiments on multi-anatomy clinical datasets, including unseen ultra-low-dose conditions as well as out-of-distribution phantom and animal datasets, demonstrate that GenDiff consistently outperforms state-of-the-art convolutional neural network and diffusion-based reconstruction methods. The proposed approach achieves superior reconstruction quality while maintaining strong robustness across different dose levels, anatomical regions, and acquisition domains, making it a promising solution for practical low-dose CT imaging.

</details>


### [3] [RegHead: Non-Humanoid Head Blendshapes via Feed-Forward Registration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12206)
*Jiahao Luo,Hao Zhang,Jianqi Chen,Yijie He,Jiaxu Zou,Michael Vasilkovsky,Sergei Korolev,Sergey Tulyakov,Chaoyang Wang,Peter Wonka,James Davis,Jian Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RegHead is a framework that constructs semantic blendshape sets for non-humanoid head avatars using a large-scale dataset, dense stochastic anchor motion representation, and a fast feed-forward registration model, enabling real-time retargeting from human face tracking signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Building semantic blendshape sets for non-humanoid head avatars remains expensive due to scarce expression-consistent supervision, lack of correspondence in generated 4D assets, and highly localized facial motion.

Method: (1) Large-scale dataset of non-humanoid identities with shared expression vocabulary via fine-tuned image editing from a small artist-rigged library; (2) Dense stochastic anchor motion representation for localized facial deformations; (3) Fast feed-forward registration model that predicts anchor-based deformations from neutral shape to convert unregistered expression meshes into corresponded blendshape basis.

Result: Produces higher-fidelity expression meshes than baselines, runs orders of magnitude faster than optimization, and achieves real-time retargeting from human face tracking signals to non-humanoid characters.

Conclusion: RegHead enables efficient, high-fidelity semantic blendshape construction for non-humanoid avatars with practical real-time retargeting capabilities.

Abstract: We present RegHead, a framework for constructing semantic blendshape sets for animatable non-humanoid head avatars. With a fixed expression vocabulary, semantic blendshapes provide a low-dimensional and interpretable animation interface and support cross-identity retargeting. Building such blendshape sets remains expensive because (i) expression-consistent supervision is scarce, (ii) generated 4D assets typically lack correspondence, and (iii) facial motion is highly localized. We propose (1) a large-scale dataset of non-humanoid identities paired with a shared expression vocabulary, obtained by expanding a small artist-rigged library via fine-tuned image editing; (2) a dense stochastic anchor motion representation tailored to localized facial deformations; and (3) a fast feed-forward registration model that converts unregistered expression meshes into a corresponded blendshape basis by predicting anchor-based deformations from the neutral shape. Experiments show that our approach produces higher-fidelity expression meshes than baselines, while running orders of magnitude faster than optimization. We further demonstrate real-time retargeting from human face tracking signals to non-humanoid characters, capturing both head pose and localized facial motions. Our project page is available at https://snap-research.github.io/RegHead/.

</details>


### [4] [Anomalous Frame Detection Using VLM-Based Description Comparison for Extracting Expert-Specific Actions and Contextual Decision-Making Scenes with Intra-Video Self-Similarity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11957)
*Ryo Sakai,Kaname Yokoyama*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a VLM-based method to detect expert know-how in task videos by extracting both expert-specific actions (via frame-level description comparisons between two videos) and contextual decision-making scenes (via intra-video segment similarity). Experiments on simulated maintenance tasks show improved extraction rates over conventional methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The declining number of skilled maintenance workers necessitates transferring expert know-how to less experienced workers. Prior work focused on extracting expert knowledge by comparing observable action differences between novice and expert videos, but expert know-how also involves contextual decision-making that is not captured by action-only comparisons.

Method: The method generates frame-wise visual descriptions using a VLM. Expert-specific actions are extracted by computing frame-level description similarities between two videos (expert vs. non-expert). Contextual decision-making scenes are extracted using intra-video segment similarities derived from self-similarity of descriptions within a video.

Result: In simulated distribution board maintenance experiments with 27 task scenarios, the proposed method achieved 65% extraction rate for action candidates and 61% for decision-scene candidates, outperforming conventional methods (59% and 33% respectively).

Conclusion: The proposed VLM-based method effectively discovers candidate scenes containing expert know-how, including both action differences and contextual decision-making, outperforming conventional approaches that only capture action-level differences.

Abstract: Maintenance of critical infrastructures, such as railways and power plants, is essential for ensuring operational safety and reliability. However, the declining number of skilled maintenance workers highlights the need to transfer expert know-how to less experienced workers. Previous studies have attempted to extract candidates of expert knowledge by comparing videos of manual-based work with those of expert workers, mainly focusing on differences in observable actions. However, expert know-how is often embedded not only in actions but also in contextual decision-making during task execution. This paper proposes a method that detects anomalous frames between two task videos to automatically extract candidate scenes containing expert-specific actions and contextual decision-making scenes. The method generates frame-wise visual descriptions using a vision-language model (VLM). Expert-specific actions are extracted based on frame similarities computed from description comparisons between two videos, while contextual decision-making scenes are extracted using segment similarities derived from intra-video self-similarity of the descriptions. In simulated distribution board maintenance experiments involving 27 task scenarios, the proposed method achieved extraction rates of 65% for action candidates and 61% for decision-scene candidates, improving over conventional methods that achieved 59% and 33%, respectively. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach in discovering candidate scenes containing expert know-how.

</details>


### [5] [Traceback Translators Against Forgetting in Continual Fake Speech Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12569)
*Enrico Gottardis,Mattia Tamiazzo,Simone Milani*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a forgetting-resilient fake speech detector using domain translators (traceback translator network) within a frozen detector to remap new feature spaces into original ones, achieving high detection rates with minimal computational effort while preserving accuracy on previous data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fake speech detectors are challenged by increasingly accurate generative models, and continual learning updates lead to catastrophic forgetting on previously seen samples.

Method: A traceback translator network is adopted as a domain translator within a frozen detector to remap new feature spaces back into the original feature space.

Result: High detection rates achieved compared to traditional retraining, with minimized computational effort and preserved detection accuracy on previous data.

Conclusion: The proposed forgetting-resilient strategy effectively addresses catastrophic forgetting in fake speech detection while maintaining efficiency and accuracy.

Abstract: Fake speech detectors are increasingly challenged by the development of new and more accurate generative models. To cope with this problem, continual learning techniques are nowadays widely considered feasible strategies for updating models to new datasets, but they also lead to decreased performance on previously seen samples (catastrophic forgetting). In this work, we propose a forgetting-resilient solution based on the adoption of domain translators within a frozen detector, which remaps the new feature spaces into the original ones by means of a traceback translator network. Experimental results show that this strategy enables the achievement of high detection rates with respect to traditional retraining, while minimizing the computational effort and preserving the detection accuracy on previous data.

</details>


### [6] [Contrastive Joint-Embedding Prediction for Representation Learning in Structural MRI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11962)
*Fabian Mager,Lars Kai Hansen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: COJEPA is a self-supervised learning framework for volumetric brain MRI that combines joint-embedding predictive architecture with contrastive loss, achieving strong results on twin retrieval, tumor segmentation, and age regression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Self-supervised learning is needed for medical imaging due to scarce labeled data; existing methods lack either local predictivity or global discriminability, motivating a combined objective.

Method: COJEPA extends I-JEPA to 3D with foreground-aware block masking, hierarchical convolutional patch embedding, and world-space sinusoidal positional encodings, trained on T1-weighted MRI from two cohorts (N=2286) using a joint-embedding predictive architecture plus contrastive loss.

Result: COJEPA achieves best monozygotic twin recall at rank@1 (0.84), best finetuning age MAE (2.55 years on OpenBHB 3.0T), and matches CO on BraTS whole-tumor Dice.

Conclusion: The combined objective yields representations that are simultaneously discriminative and locally structured, outperforming or matching individual objectives across diverse downstream tasks.

Abstract: Self-supervised learning offers a compelling approach for medical imaging, where labeled data are scarce and acquisition costs are high. We present COJEPA, a self-supervised framework for volumetric brain MRI that combines a joint-embedding predictive architecture (JEPA) with a contrastive loss (CO), targeting two complementary properties: local predictivity and global discriminability. The model is trained without labels on T1-weighted structural MRI from two cohorts (HCP-YA and AABC, $N{=}2286$, ages 22 to 90), extending I-JEPA to 3D with foreground-aware block masking, a hierarchical convolutional patch embedding, and world-space sinusoidal positional encodings. We evaluate all three objectives across zero-shot twin retrieval, brain tumor segmentation (BraTS 2024), and age regression (OpenBHB). COJEPA achieves the best monozygotic twin recall at rank@1 (0.84), the best finetuning age MAE (2.55 years on OpenBHB 3.0T), and matches CO on BraTS whole-tumor Dice, demonstrating that the combined objective yields representations that are simultaneously discriminative and locally structured.

</details>


### [7] [SpikeDS: Dual Sparsity Spikformer for Perineural Invasion Prediction in 3D MRI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11986)
*Induk Um,Youngung Han,Kyeonghun Kim,Yului Jeong,Jina Jeong,Hyunsu Go,Dohyun Kweon,Sungha Park,Junga Kim,Anna Jung,Suah Park,Hyuk-Jae Lee,Pa Hong,Woo Kyoung Jeong,Won Jae Lee,Ken Ying-Kai Liao,Nam-Joon Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SpikeDS is a spiking neural network that uses dual sparsity (activation sparsity from binary spikes and spatial sparsity from window pruning) to detect perineural invasion in cholangiocarcinoma from 3D MRI, achieving AUC 0.753 with only 14.4 mJ energy consumption.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Perineural invasion (PNI) detection in cholangiocarcinoma (CCA) from 3D MRI is clinically important but challenging due to subtle/spatially heterogeneous signatures; existing deep learning methods incur prohibitive computational costs on volumetric medical images, limiting clinical deployment.

Method: Proposes SpikeDS with Dual Sparsity Spiking Attention (DSSA) combining: (1) Window-based Expert Mixture Spiking Attention (W-EMSA) that selectively attends to salient windows based on firing rates, and (2) Cross-Window Spiking Self-Attention (CW-SSA) enabling global context via asymmetric scheme where pruned windows remain key-value sources.

Result: On 139 CCA patients with 5-fold cross-validation, SpikeDS achieves AUC 0.753 with only 14.4 mJ energy consumption, outperforming baselines in both AUC and energy efficiency.

Conclusion: Dual sparsity is an effective hardware-aware strategy for improving 3D spiking transformer efficiency without compromising diagnostic performance.

Abstract: Perineural invasion (PNI) is associated with poor prognosis in cholangiocarcinoma (CCA). However, its detection from 3D MRI remains challenging due to the subtle and spatially heterogeneous imaging signatures at the tumor periphery. Capturing such spatially sparse cues necessitates volumetric analysis of 3D MRI, but existing deep learning approaches incur prohibitive computational costs on volumetric medical images, limiting their clinical deployment. We propose Dual Sparsity Spikformer (SpikeDS), a spiking neural network architecture that jointly exploits activation sparsity from binary spike communication and spatial sparsity from window pruning based on firing rates. SpikeDS introduces Dual Sparsity Spiking Attention (DSSA), which combines two complementary mechanisms. The first is Window-based Expert Mixture Spiking Attention (W-EMSA), which selectively applies attention only to salient windows identified by their firing rates. The second is Cross-Window Spiking Self-Attention (CW-SSA), which enables global context exchange through an asymmetric scheme in which pruned windows still contribute as key-value sources. Evaluated on a clinical cohort of 139 CCA patients via 5-fold cross-validation, SpikeDS achieves an AUC of 0.753 while consuming only 14.4 mJ, surpassing the best baseline in both AUC and energy efficiency. These results suggest that dual sparsity provides an effective hardware-aware strategy for improving the efficiency of 3D spiking transformers without compromising diagnostic performance.

</details>


### [8] [Anatomy-Privileged Distillation with Token Routing for MRI-Based Prediction of Perineural Invasion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11987)
*Hyunsu Go,Youngung Han,Kyeonghun Kim,Junga Kim,Dohyun Kweon,Jinyong Jun,Sungha Park,Anna Jung,Induk Um,Yului Jeong,Suah Park,Jina Jeong,Pa Hong,Woo Kyoung Jeong,Won Jae Lee,Ken Ying-Kai Liao,Hyuk-Jae Lee,Nam-Joon Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A teacher-student framework using T2-weighted MRI predicts perineural invasion in intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma patients. The teacher uses anatomical masks to learn token routing; the student distills this knowledge without needing masks at inference. Achieves 0.750 mean AUROC with efficient computation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Perineural invasion (PNI) is a key pathological factor for poor postoperative outcomes in intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma, but it is only confirmed post-surgery. Existing preoperative MRI-based models rely on radiologist-defined variables, contrast-enhanced imaging, or manual annotations, limiting automation and generalizability.

Method: An anatomy-privileged teacher-student framework: (1) Teacher network uses T2-weighted MRI with tumor and liver masks to learn dense token routing. (2) Student network distills this guidance to retain and aggregate informative tokens under a fixed budget. Anatomical supervision is used only during training; no masks are required at inference.

Result: Evaluated on 155 patients, the method achieved the highest mean AUROC of 0.750 among matched MRI-only baselines under the same protocol, with computational efficiency of 1.43 GFLOPs and 8.02 ms per case on a Jetson Orin Nano Super Developer Kit.

Conclusion: The proposed framework enables efficient, mask-free patient-level PNI prediction from T2-weighted MRI, outperforming existing MRI-only baselines while maintaining low computational cost suitable for edge deployment.

Abstract: Perineural invasion (PNI) is associated with poor postoperative outcomes in intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma, but it is confirmed by surgical pathology. Existing preoperative imaging models often rely on radiologist-defined variables, contrast-enhanced imaging, or manual annotations. We propose an anatomy-privileged teacher--student framework for patient-level PNI prediction from T2-weighted MRI. During training, the teacher uses MRI with tumor and liver masks to learn dense token routing, and the student distills this guidance to retain and aggregate informative tokens under a fixed budget. Anatomical supervision is restricted to training, and the deployed model does not require masks at inference. In 155 patients, the proposed method achieved the highest mean AUROC of 0.750 among matched MRI-only baselines evaluated under the same protocol, with 1.43 GFLOPs and 8.02 ms per case on a Jetson Orin Nano Super Developer Kit.

</details>


### [9] [MetaView: Monocular Novel View Synthesis with Scale-Aware Implicit Geometry Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12000)
*Yufei Cai,Xuesong Niu,Hao Lu,Kun Gai,Kai Wu,Guosheng Lin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MetaView is a diffusion-based monocular novel view synthesis framework that combines implicit geometry modeling with metric depth cues to achieve geometry consistency and precise camera control under large viewpoint changes from a single image, significantly outperforming existing methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing visual generation models lack coherent spatial structure perception. Generative novel view synthesis with explicit geometry priors restricts generalization under large view changes, while implicit methods offer flexibility but lack precise camera control and geometry consistency. The goal is to achieve both geometry consistency and controllability under large viewpoint changes from a single image.

Method: MetaView uses a diffusion-based framework that combines implicit geometry priors from a feed-forward geometry perception network (for structure regularization without restrictive reconstruction pipelines) with metric depth (to anchor generation to a metric scale), enabling both geometry consistency and precise camera control.

Result: Under challenging monocular large viewpoint changes, MetaView significantly outperforms existing methods and exhibits superior generalization.

Conclusion: Combining implicit geometry modeling with minimal explicit 3D cues (metric depth) is an effective design for monocular novel view synthesis, achieving both geometry consistency and precise controllability under large view changes.

Abstract: Current visual generation models are capable of producing high-quality content, yet they lack a coherent perception of the spatial structure. Existing generative novel view synthesis methods typically introduce explicit geometry priors, which enforce spatial consistency but inherently restrict generalization in large view changes. In contrast, recent interactive generative methods favor implicit scene modeling, offering greater flexibility at the cost of precise camera control and geometry consistency. In this paper, we propose MetaView, a diffusion-based monocular novel view synthesis framework that enables rendering under large view changes from a single image. Our key insight is to combine implicit geometry modeling with minimal yet essential explicit 3D cues: we incorporate implicit geometry priors from a feed-forward geometry perception network to regularize structure without imposing restrictive reconstruction pipelines, while leveraging metric depth to anchor the generation to a metric scale. This design allows MetaView to achieve both geometry consistency and precise controllability. Extensive experiments demonstrate that, under challenging monocular large viewpoint changes, MetaView significantly outperforms existing methods and exhibits superior generalization. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/KlingAIResearch/MetaView.

</details>


### [10] [SymbOmni: Evolving Agentic Omni Models via Symbolic Concept Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12042)
*Jinxiu Liu,Jianru Li,Tanqing Kuang,Xuanming Liu,Kangfu Mei,Yandong Wen,Weiyang Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SymbOmni is an agentic omni-model that addresses the "perpetual novice" problem in visual generation by using Symbolic Concept Learning—a memory module that abstracts low-level operations into reusable symbolic instructions, enabling cumulative evolution via an induction-transduction cycle and verbalized backpropagation, outperforming existing systems in image quality, task success, token efficiency, and continual learning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current visual generation models suffer from the "perpetual novice" problem: they cannot learn cumulatively or evolve autonomously, lack mechanisms to structure experience into reusable knowledge, and rely on brittle from-scratch reasoning per task, leading to poor compositional generalization and inefficient knowledge retention.

Method: SymbOmni uses a Symbolic Concept Box—an optimizable memory module that abstracts low-level operations into reusable Symbolic Workflow Instructions. It operates via an induction-transduction cycle: experiences are abstracted into symbolic concepts (induction) and adaptively composed for novel tasks (transduction). Training uses verbalized backpropagation with language-based feedback, avoiding gradient-based fine-tuning.

Result: (I) Outperforms existing agent-based systems and closed-source models (e.g., Nano Banana, GPT-Image-1) in image quality and task success; (II) reduces token consumption by over 40% while maintaining competitive quality; (III) achieves cumulative gains across online-learning benchmarks, setting a new state of the art.

Conclusion: SymbOmni demonstrates that symbolic concept learning with verbalized backpropagation enables effective cumulative evolution and continual learning in visual generation, overcoming fundamental limitations of monolithic models.

Abstract: Visual generation is increasingly ubiquitous in diverse domains, from text-to-image/video synthesis to multimodal interactive creation. Yet prevailing monolithic models remain fundamentally constrained by their inability to learn cumulatively and evolve autonomously, which is a limitation we term the "perpetual novice" problem. They lack mechanisms for structuring experience into reusable knowledge and therefore rely on brittle, "from-scratch" reasoning for each task, resulting in poor compositional generalization and inefficient knowledge retention. Motivated by these limitations, we propose SymbOmni, an agentic omni-model designed for cumulative evolution through Symbolic Concept Learning. At its core is the Symbolic Concept Box, an optimizable memory module that abstracts low-level operations into reusable Symbolic Workflow Instructions. SymbOmni operates through an induction-transduction cycle: experiences are abstracted into symbolic concepts (induction), which are then adaptively composed to solve novel tasks (transduction). The training is done by verbalized backpropagation with language-based feedback to enable continuous self-improvement without gradient-based model fine-tuning. Comprehensive experiments validate that (I) SymbOmni significantly outperforms existing agent-based systems for iterative creation and also surpasses closed-source models (e.g., Nano Banana, GPT-Image-1) in both image quality and task success rates; (II) SymbOmni effectively reduces token consumption by over 40% while maintaining competitive generation quality; and (III) SymbOmni enables effective continual learning by achieving cumulative gains across multiple online-learning benchmarks and setting a new state of the art.

</details>


### [11] [An Empirical Analysis of Continual Learning for Heterogeneous Medical Visual Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12048)
*Mai A. Shaaban,Tausifa Jan Saleem,Alaa Mohamed,Dilnaz Utemissova,Ufaq Khan,Mohammad Yaqub*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper systematically evaluates continual learning methods for medical VQA across diverse clinical tasks, finding that existing CL methods struggle to maintain stability-plasticity balance when heterogeneous tasks are interleaved.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deploying MedVQA in real-world clinical settings requires models that adapt to new tasks without forgetting prior knowledge, yet the behavior of continual learning methods when training across heterogeneous MedVQA tasks remains underexplored.

Method: Systematic evaluation of CL methods for MedVQA across diverse clinical objectives (classification, multi-label classification, detection, cell counting, report generation), examining: (1) catastrophic forgetting mitigation; (2) sensitivity to task ordering; (3) evolution of low-rank adaptation parameters and weight drift patterns.

Result: Existing CL methods struggle to maintain stability-plasticity balance when tasks with different objectives and supervision formats are interleaved.

Conclusion: Current continual learning approaches are insufficient for heterogeneous MedVQA task sequences, highlighting the need for better CL methods that handle diverse clinical objectives and supervision formats.

Abstract: Deploying medical visual question answering (MedVQA) systems in real-world clinical settings requires models that adapt to new clinical tasks without forgetting previously acquired knowledge. Continual learning (CL) provides a practical framework for this setting. Despite rapid progress in medical vision-language models, the behavior of CL methods when training these models across heterogeneous MedVQA tasks remains underexplored. This work presents a systematic evaluation of CL for MedVQA across diverse clinical objectives, including classification, multi-label classification, detection, cell counting, and report generation. Specifically, we explore (1) the ability of existing CL methods to mitigate catastrophic forgetting; (2) their sensitivity to task ordering, analyzing how different task sequences influence performance retention and forgetting; and (3) the evolution of low-rank adaptation parameters as new tasks are learned, revealing patterns of weight drift under different CL methods. Our findings suggest that existing CL methods struggle to maintain stability-plasticity balance when tasks with different objectives and supervision formats are interleaved. Code and full experimental setup will be publicly available.

</details>


### [12] [Representation and Reference Selection in Training-Free Synthetic Image Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12052)
*Meiling Li,Pietro Bongini,Benedetta Tondi,Mauro Barni*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Training-free reference-based synthetic image attribution is influenced by the interaction between representation space (intermediate layers of CLIP/DINOv2 perform best) and reference selection method (semantically aligned references improve accuracy-cost trade-off, while resynthesis helps under low-reference budgets).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The interaction between representation space and reference construction in training-free reference-based attribution remains largely unexplored. This paper aims to provide a controlled analysis of this interaction.

Method: Controlled analysis using off-the-shelf pretrained representations (CLIP, DINOv2) across different layers, combined with three reference selection methods: arbitrary, semantically aligned, and resynthesis-based references.

Result: Attribution accuracy peaks at intermediate representation levels. Semantically constrained references reduce query-reference mismatch. Resynthesis is most useful in low-reference regimes, while semantically aligned references offer better accuracy-cost trade-off with moderate reference pools.

Conclusion: Training-free reference-based attribution should be understood as the interaction between where images are compared, how the reference set is constructed, and how many references are available.

Abstract: Synthetic image attribution aims at identifying the generator responsible for a given AI-generated image. Training-free reference-based attribution methods are easily scalable, since newly emerging generators can be incorporated by adding source-specific references rather than retraining a task-specific classifier. Their performance depends on two coupled factors: the representation space used for comparison and the way source-specific references are constructed. However, the interaction between these two factors remains largely unexplored. In this paper, we provide a controlled analysis of this interaction using references and off-the-shelf pretrained representations. We study representations extracted from different layers of CLIP and DINOv2, along with three reference selection methods with varying semantic constraints: arbitrary, semantically aligned, and resynthesis-based references. Our results show that attribution accuracy consistently peaks at intermediate representation levels, indicating that source-discriminative cues are more accessible before strong semantic abstraction dominates. We further show that intermediate representations are not completely semantically neutral, making reference selection critical: semantically constrained references reduce query-reference mismatch and improve attribution, especially under limited reference budgets. Resynthesis is most useful in low-reference regimes, while semantically aligned references provide a better accuracy-cost trade-off when a moderate-sized reference pool is available. Our findings show that training-free reference-based attribution should be understood as the interaction between where images are compared, how the reference set is constructed, and how many references are available.

</details>


### [13] [Learning from Complementary Ultrasound Representations for Liver Disease Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12062)
*Sabahattin Mert Daloglu,Gokce Bekar,Ceren Coskun,Senanur Sahin,Harvey Castro,Soner Hacihaliloglu,Halley P. Letter,Ilker Hacihaliloglu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This study improves NASH vs. NAFLD classification by combining conventional B-mode ultrasound with physics-guided and local phase-based image representations, using self-supervised MAEs and GCNs. On a multi-site Mayo Clinic cohort of 2,547 scans, complementary representations achieved up to 32.4% higher accuracy and 91.2% higher F1-score over B-mode alone, with consistent gains across demographic subgroups.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Differentiating NASH from NAFLD using ultrasound is clinically important but challenging due to subtle tissue differences and limited information in conventional B-mode imaging.

Method: Combine conventional B-mode ultrasound with physics-guided and local phase-based image representations; evaluated using self-supervised masked autoencoders (MAEs) and graph convolutional networks (GCNs) on a multi-site Mayo Clinic cohort (2,547 scans from 125 patients).

Result: Complementary ultrasound representations consistently improved classification over B-mode alone, with gains up to 32.4% in accuracy and 91.2% in F1-score. Improvements were consistent across age, sex, race, ethnicity, and acquisition sites.

Conclusion: Adding complementary ultrasound representations derived from the same acquisition significantly enhances NASH vs. NAFLD classification and generalizes well across diverse patient subgroups.

Abstract: Differentiating non-alcoholic steatohepatitis (NASH) from non-alcoholic fatty liver disease (NAFLD) using ultrasound remains challenging due to subtle tissue alterations and the limited information available in conventional B-mode imaging. In this work, we investigate whether complementary ultrasound representations derived from the same acquisition can improve NASH versus NAFLD classification. Specifically, we combine conventional B-mode ultrasound with physics-guided and local phase-based image representations and evaluate their effectiveness using self-supervised masked autoencoders (MAEs) and graph convolutional networks (GCNs). Experiments were conducted on a multi-site Mayo Clinic cohort consisting of 2,547 liver ultrasound scans from 125 patients. Compared with conventional B-mode ultrasound alone, complementary ultrasound representations consistently improved classification performance, yielding gains of up to 32.4% in accuracy and 91.2% in F1-score. Furthermore, performance improvements were consistently observed across age groups, sex, race, ethnicity,and acquisition sites.

</details>


### [14] [NEEDL-Bench: Dataset for Swiss Needle Cast and Stomata Detection in Microscopy Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12076)
*Benjamin Blake,Declan McIntosh,Jürgen Ehlting,Nicolas Feau,Joey B. Tanney,Alexandra Branzan Albu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: NEEDL-Bench is a microscopy detection benchmark for Swiss Needle Cast disease in Douglas-fir trees, comprising 3250 annotated images across two evaluation splits, with baseline F1 scores of 0.8479, revealing that larger models do not proportionally improve performance, indicating domain-specific inductive biases are needed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: There is no existing dataset for automatic computer vision detection of pseudothecia (SNC reproductive structures) in Douglas-fir needles, despite computer vision's potential to standardize and scale severity measurements for this ecologically and economically important disease.

Method: The authors collected 3250 annotated images from 1082 Douglas-fir needles, annotated for both keypoint and bounding-box detectors. They created two evaluation splits (random sampling and sequential sampling for diversity) and evaluated multiple keypoint and bounding box detection methods as baselines.

Result: Maximum F1 score of 0.8479 was achieved; larger models did not show commensurate performance gains on this dataset.

Conclusion: Improvements on this problem will not come from scaling laws but rather from domain-specific inductive biases, suggesting significant potential for future development.

Abstract: We present NEEDL-Bench, a microscopy detection benchmark for Swiss Needle Cast (SNC), a fungal disease of Douglas-fir trees. Douglas-fir is a keystone species of major ecological and economic importance as a softwood timber resource, and SNC affects productivity by forming sexual reproductive structures (pseudothecia) that emerge through the gas exchange pores (stomata) of the needles, thereby blocking gas exchange and compromising needle function. To date, there is no dataset for automatic computer vision detection of these structures, despite computer vision being well poised to standardize and viably scale severity measurements. To address this, we present NEEDL-Bench, a dataset of 3250 annotated images from 1082 Douglas-fir needles, annotated for both keypoints and bounding-box detectors. This dataset exhibits a challenging collection of features, including blur, poor object contrast, small objects of interest, and occlusions. To better capture both the nominal distribution of the data and the full breadth of rare structures, we present two distinct evaluation splits: either random sampling from the collected images or sequential sampling to maximize structural diversity. We evaluate multiple popular keypoint and bounding box methods for detection on this dataset as a baseline and observe a maximum F1 score of 0.8479, suggesting significant potential for gains from future development on this problem. Further, we find that larger models generally do not show commensurate gains in performance on this dataset, indicating that improvements on this problem will not come from scaling laws but rather from domain-specific inductive biases.

</details>


### [15] [Causal Supervision of Attention for Affective Behaviour Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12091)
*Nemanja Rašajski,Konstantinos Makantasis,Antonios Liapis,Georgios N. Yannakakis*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes an attention pooling framework for multi-task affective behavior analysis (Valence-Arousal estimation, Expression Recognition, Action Unit Detection) using causal supervision, cross-covariance independence regularization, and SwiGLU transformation to improve generalization beyond spurious correlations, achieving competitive scores on the s-Aff-Wild2 database.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing attention mechanisms in affective behavior analysis can exploit dataset-specific correlations instead of true emotion-related cues, leading to poor generalization across subjects with varying identity, illumination, pose, and demographics.

Method: (1) Causal supervision to enforce attention on facial regions with invariant predictive value across subjects; (2) Cross-covariance independence regularization between Key (K) and Value (V) projections to encourage non-redundant representations; (3) Gated nonlinear SwiGLU transformation replacing linear Value projection for finer-grained feature capture.

Result: CCC_VA=0.5123, F1_EX=0.3116, F1_AU=0.3974, overall P score=1.2214 on the official validation set of s-Aff-Wild2.

Conclusion: The proposed attention pooling framework with subject-invariant attention and enhanced feature expressiveness improves generalization and achieves competitive multi-task performance in affective behavior analysis.

Abstract: Affective Behaviour Analysis aims to enable machines to infer human affective states from behavioural signals, particularly facial expressions, in real-world environments. The \textit{11th Affective Behaviour Analysis in-the-wild Competition} includes the Multi-Task Learning Challenge based on the s-Aff-Wild2 database, where participants develop a unified framework for Valence-Arousal Estimation, Expression Recognition, and Action Unit Detection. This is challenging because emotion-related cues must be distinguished from spurious factors such as identity, illumination, pose, and demographic variation. Attention mechanisms are well suited as they aggregate information from the most informative facial regions, but may still exploit dataset-specific correlations instead of true affective cues. To improve generalization, we propose an attention pooling framework that promotes subject-invariant attention while increasing feature expressiveness. Our method consists of three components. First, we introduce causal supervision to enforce attention on facial regions with invariant predictive value across subjects. Second, we apply a cross-covariance independence regularization between Key (K) and Value (V) projections to encourage complementary, non-redundant representations. Finally, we replace the linear Value projection with a gated nonlinear SwiGLU transformation to increase feature expressiveness and capture finer-grained affective cues. Our method achieves $CCC_{VA}=0.5123$ for VA estimation on the official validation set, together with $F1_{EX}=0.3116$ and $F1_{AU}=0.3974$ for expression recognition and action unit detection, respectively, resulting in an overall $P$ score (the sum of the individual task metrics) of $1.2214$.

</details>


### [16] [ACZ-GSeg: Adaptive Concentric Zone-based Two-stage Ground Segmentation for LiDAR Point Clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12110)
*Ge Zhang Chunyang Wang Bin Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A two-stage ground segmentation method using Adaptive Concentric Zone Model that dynamically balances point distribution, achieving 97.66% F1-score on SemanticKITTI and 99.36% on self-collected data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing ground segmentation methods suffer from under-segmentation of ground points due to sparse long-range point clouds, ground undulations, and interference from non-ground structures in complex road scenarios.

Method: Proposes Adaptive Concentric Zone Model (dynamically determines sector counts per ring) with two-stage segmentation: (1) coarse segmentation uses lowest-height seed constraint and height-decay weighted PCA plane fitting; (2) fine segmentation uses reflectance intensity consistency constraint and local height stability refinement.

Result: On SemanticKITTI: Precision 99.12%, Recall 96.24%, F1-score 97.66%. On self-collected RUBY-PLUS data: Precision 98.72%, Recall 100.00%, F1-score 99.36%.

Conclusion: The method effectively adapts to LiDAR's range-dependent density distribution (dense near, sparse far), reduces non-ground misclassification while maintaining ground recall, and improves ground segmentation stability.

Abstract: Ground segmentation is a fundamental prerequisite for autonomous navigation, environmental perception, and object detection in ground mobile platforms. To address the under-segmentation of ground points caused by sparse long-range point clouds, ground undulations, and interference from non-ground structures in complex road scenarios, this paper proposes a two-stage ground segmentation method based on the Adaptive Concentric Zone Model. First, an Adaptive Concentric Zone Model is constructed to dynamically determine the number of sectors in each ring, thereby forming local zones with more balanced point distributions. Based on this model, a two-stage ground segmentation method is developed. In the coarse segmentation stage, a lowest-height seed constraint and height-decay weighting are introduced to establish a weighted principal component analysis plane fitting model, from which ground candidate points are extracted. In the fine segmentation stage, a reflectance intensity consistency constraint is employed to distinguish high-confidence ground points from uncertain points, and the uncertain points are further refined based on the local height stability of high-confidence neighborhoods. Experimental results show that the proposed method achieves Precision, Recall, and F1-score values of 99.12%, 96.24%, and 97.66% on the SemanticKITTI dataset, and 98.72%, 100.00%, and 99.36%, respectively, on a self-collected point cloud acquired using a RUBY-PLUS. The results demonstrate that the proposed method can effectively adapt to the range-dependent distribution characteristics of LiDAR point clouds, which are dense at near ranges and sparse at far ranges. It reduces the misclassification of non-ground points while maintaining ground point recall, thereby effectively improving the stability of ground segmentation.

</details>


### [17] [Data Safety: Synthetic Data Quality Analysis Using CIFAKE Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12165)
*Kuniko Paxton,Amila Akagić,Koorosh Aslansefat,Dhavalkumar Thakker,Yiannis Papadopoulos*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper systematically analyzes differences between synthetic and real images for training classification models, examining high-dimensional features, low-level color statistics, and training processes. It proposes an evaluation and application strategy for safely incorporating synthetic data of unknown quality into training through realistic data mixing scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-performance image classification models require vast training data, but real images are often impractical to obtain. While synthetic data is used as a substitute, synthetic images are not necessarily equivalent to real images for training, necessitating a systematic understanding of their differences and optimal utilization strategies.

Method: Systematic analysis from three perspectives: high-dimensional feature space, low-level statistics in color space, and model training process. Additionally, experimental verification using realistic data mixing scenarios to determine how synthetic data should be utilized.

Result: An evaluation and application strategy that enables preliminary assessment of synthetic images of unknown quality and allows safe incorporation into training, with experimentally verified data mixing approaches.

Conclusion: The proposed strategy contributes to enhancing the reliability and safety of image classification models when utilizing synthetic images as training data.

Abstract: Recently, the societal implementation of high-performance image classification models has expanded rapidly. While these models require vast amounts of training data to improve performance, securing sufficient real images is often impractical. As a means to compensate for this shortage, the use of synthetic data is becoming widespread. However, synthetic images are not necessarily equivalent to real images for training purposes. This study systematically analyzes the differences between two types of synthetic images created by different generation methods and real images from three perspectives: high-dimensional feature space, low-level statistics in color space, and the model training process. Furthermore, it experimentally verifies how synthetic data should be utilized by considering realistic data mixing scenarios. This enables the proposal of an evaluation and application strategy for performing preliminary assessments on synthetic images of unknown quality and safely incorporating them into training. This research aims to contribute to enhancing the reliability and safety of image classification models utilizing synthetic images.

</details>


### [18] [Self-Consistent Flow: Unifying Velocity and Endpoint Prediction for Rectified Flow Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12171)
*Xu Han,Jiajing Hu,Li-Ping Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SC-Flow unifies velocity and data endpoint predictions in rectified-flow models via a consistency loss, improving generation quality and path straightness with minimal overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work shows that different parameterizations (predicting velocity vs. data endpoint) lead to different empirical behaviors, but their respective advantages are underexplored, and how to combine them effectively remains unclear.

Method: Proposes Self-Consistent Flow (SC-Flow), which jointly trains a single network to predict both local velocity and data endpoint using a lightweight consistency loss, without major architectural changes.

Result: SC-Flow substantially stabilizes optimization, improves straightness of generation paths, and yields significant gains in generation quality over standard rectified-flow baselines on image generation tasks.

Conclusion: Predicting data endpoint stabilizes training while predicting velocity maintains stable sampling near the data manifold; SC-Flow unifies these benefits effectively.

Abstract: In rectified-flow-based generative models, the neural network can be trained to predict two different targets, such as the instantaneous velocity or the data endpoint, to perform denoising. Although prior work shows that these parameterizations lead to different empirical behaviors, the mechanisms underlying their respective advantages remain to be underexplored, and how to combine them effectively is still unclear. In this work, we analyze how learning errors from different parameterizations affect the generation performance. We show that predicting the data endpoint has a clear training signal that stabilizes training, whereas predicting the velocity maintains stable sampling dynamics near the data manifold. Motivated by these insights, we propose Self-Consistent Flow (SC-Flow), a new method that unifies the benefits of both parameterizations. By employing a lightweight consistency loss, SC-Flow jointly trains a single network to predict both the local velocity and the data endpoint, and the consistency between the two predictions improves the model's performance. The method requires no major architectural changes and adds minimal computational overhead. Extensive experiments on image generation tasks demonstrate that SC-Flow substantially stabilizes optimization and improves the straightness of generation paths, leading to significant gains in generation quality over standard rectified-flow baselines.

</details>


### [19] [From Reconstruction to Interpretation: Zero-Setup Multi-Phase Segmentation of X-ray Tomography Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12175)
*Pradyumna Elavarthi,Arun J. Bhattacharjee,Harrison Lisabeth,Anca Ralescu,Petrus H. Zwart,Dilworth Parkinson,Elizabeth G. Clark*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents a zero-setup, material-agnostic deep learning framework for multi-phase segmentation of synchrotron X-ray tomography data that generates interpretable masks without user input or retraining, enabling near-real-time beamline feedback and scalable scientific imaging workflows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rapid interpretation of X-ray tomography data is limited by image segmentation, which often requires manual thresholding, user prompting, or material-specific model training, hindering real-time feedback during beamline experiments.

Method: Combines a material-agnostic mask preparation strategy with a pretrained semantic segmentation network to generate six interpretable region masks (background, sample, bright, dark-gray, light-gray, porosity) without dataset-specific annotations or retraining.

Result: Consistent and physically meaningful segmentations across varying samples and imaging conditions on held-out synchrotron micro-CT images and qualitative datasets; substantially outperforms conventional intensity-based thresholding.

Conclusion: The framework enables rapid diagnostic-level segmentation within minutes of reconstruction, supporting near-real-time beamline feedback and scalable AI-assisted scientific imaging, with optional manual refinement or fine-tuning for application-specific needs.

Abstract: X-ray tomography enables nondestructive characterization of material microstructures, while advances in micro-CT imaging have accelerated volumetric data acquisition and reconstruction. However, rapid interpretation remains limited by image segmentation, which often requires manual thresholding, user prompting, or material-specific model training. We present a zero-setup framework for multi-phase segmentation of synchrotron X-ray tomography data that generates interpretable masks for previously unseen datasets without user input or retraining during deployment. The framework combines a material-agnostic mask preparation strategy with a pretrained semantic segmentation network. It represents commonly occurring structural regions as background, sample, bright, dark-gray, light-gray, and porosity masks. Unlike conventional deep learning pipelines that require dataset-specific annotations and retraining, the proposed framework can be applied directly to new scans and produce diagnostic-level segmentations within minutes of reconstruction. This enables rapid assessment of scan quality, sample morphology, porosity, and attenuation variations during ongoing beamline experiments. The generated masks can later be manually refined or used to fine-tune application-specific models when greater accuracy or material-specific labeling is required. Evaluation on held-out synchrotron micro-CT images and qualitative testing on additional datasets demonstrate consistent and physically meaningful segmentations across varying samples and imaging conditions. The framework also substantially outperforms conventional intensity-based thresholding. By connecting high-speed reconstruction with immediate interpretation, the approach supports near-real-time beamline feedback and scalable AI-assisted scientific imaging workflows.

</details>


### [20] [A Calibrated Multimodal Ensemble for Ambivalence/Hesitancy Recognition: System Description and Private-Test Submission Strategy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12176)
*Josep Cabacas-Maso,Ismael Benito-Altamirano,Carles Ventura*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A calibrated ensemble of three fusion models over frozen face, audio, text, and pose embeddings achieves 0.7358 macro-F1 on public test and 0.7361 on private test for recognizing ambivalence and hesitancy from video, demonstrating strong generalization to unseen participants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Ambivalence and hesitancy (A/H) undermine digital behaviour-change interventions, and automatic recognition from video is needed to address the ABAW A/H challenge on the BAH dataset.

Method: Calibrated, equal-weight ensemble of three fusion models operating over frozen face, audio, text, and pose embeddings.

Result: 0.7358 macro-F1 on public test set; 0.7361 macro-F1 on private test set (30 new participants), closely matching public-test estimate.

Conclusion: The pipeline generalizes to unseen participants without leakage, as evidenced by nearly identical public and private test scores.

Abstract: Ambivalence and hesitancy (A/H) undermine digital behaviour-change interventions, and recognizing them automatically from video is the goal of the ABAW A/H challenge on the BAH dataset. We describe our system for the 11th edition of the challenge: a calibrated, equal-weight ensemble of three fusion models over frozen face, audio, text, and pose embeddings, which reaches 0.7358 macro-F1 on the public test set. This year's private test, released on a disjoint set of 30 new participants, is scored on five allowed submissions; we report the configuration and rationale of each of our five submissions, and, where already available, the private-test score obtained. Our first submission, an exact replica of the calibrated ensemble tuned only on public validation, scored 0.7361 macro-F1 on the private test, matching our public-test estimate almost exactly and confirming the pipeline generalizes to unseen participants without leakage.

</details>


### [21] [Overview of Cross-Component In-loop Filters in Video Coding Standards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12186)
*Zhaoyu Li,Xuewei Meng,Jiaqi Zhang,Cheng Huang,Chuanmin Jia,Siwei Ma,Yun Jiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper reviews cross-component filters (CC-ALF and CCSAO) in modern video coding standards, which improve chroma fidelity by leveraging luma-chroma correlations to reduce compression artifacts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional in-loop filters process each color component separately, limiting chroma quality improvement. Cross-component filters exploit correlations between luma and chroma channels to enhance chroma fidelity.

Method: The paper provides a comprehensive survey of cross-component filters, specifically Cross-Component Adaptive Loop Filter (CC-ALF) and Cross-Component Sample Adaptive Offset (CCSAO), tracing their origin, development, and current status in video coding standards.

Result: Cross-component filters effectively reduce compression artifacts by utilizing inter-component correlations, yielding more accurate pixel reconstruction values compared to component-independent filtering.

Conclusion: The paper concludes with discussions on future evolution directions for cross-component filters in advancing video coding standards.

Abstract: In-loop filters have been comprehensively explored during the development of video coding standards due to their remarkable noise-reduction capability. In the early stage of video coding, in-loop filters, such as Deblocking Filter, Sample Adaptive Offset, and Adaptive Loop Filter, were performed separately for each component. Recently, cross-component filters were studied to improve the chroma fidelity by exploiting correlations between the luma and chroma channels. This paper summarizes the cross-component filters used in the state-of-the-art video coding standard. Specifically, it includes the Cross-Component Adaptive Loop Filter and Cross-Component Sample Adaptive Offset. Cross-component filters aim to reduce compression artifacts based on the correlation between different components and provide more accurate pixel reconstruction values. In this paper, we introduce the origin, development, and status of cross-component filters in the current video coding standards. Finally, we had some discussions on the further evolutions of cross-component filters.

</details>


### [22] [Beyond Perfect Priors: Adaptive Gaussian Graph for 4D Driving Reconstruction in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12214)
*Xiaoyun Dong,Qian Xu,Yun Wang,Yang Lu,Jen-Ming Wu,Jianping Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AGG is a self-correcting 4D Gaussian Graph framework that robustly reconstructs driving scenes from in-the-wild videos by using a Semantically-Guided Tick-Tock Strategy to decouple background/camera from dynamic agents and an Adaptive Topology Evolution module to rectify graph structures, outperforming SOTA on KITTI and the new Wild-30 benchmark under noisy priors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing Gaussian Scene Graph methods fail on in-the-wild videos due to optimization ambiguity (e.g., entangled camera and agent poses) and topological failures (e.g., missing objects) when initialized with noisy priors like inaccurate camera poses, LiDAR depth, or manual annotations.

Method: 1) Semantically-Guided Tick-Tock Strategy: leverages 2D foundation features to explicitly decouple static background and camera pose updates from dynamic agent learning. 2) Adaptive Topology Evolution: actively rectifies graph structures by spawning missing agents, reassigning misclassified Gaussians, and pruning false positives.

Result: Consistently outperforms state-of-the-art approaches in visual fidelity and robustness under noisy priors on both KITTI and the new Wild-30 benchmark (internet and generative videos).

Conclusion: AGG enables robust in-the-wild 4D driving scene reconstruction without relying on precise priors, making it suitable for diverse autonomous driving simulation scenarios.

Abstract: Reconstructing 4D driving scenes in the wild (e.g., internet and AI-generated videos) is critical for diverse autonomous driving simulation. While recent Gaussian Scene Graph (GSG) methods achieve impressive visual quality, they heavily rely on precise priors, such as accurate camera poses and LiDAR depth, or manual annotations. When initialized with noisy priors estimated from in-the-wild videos, existing GSG methods suffer from optimization ambiguity (e.g., entangling camera and agent poses) and topological failures (e.g., missing objects), causing severe rendering artifacts. To enable robust in-the-wild reconstruction, we introduce Adaptive Gaussian Graph (AGG), a self-correcting 4D framework. Our Semantically-Guided Tick-Tock Strategy leverages 2D foundation features to explicitly decouple static background and camera pose updates from dynamic agent learning. Concurrently, our Adaptive Topology Evolution module actively rectifies graph structures by spawning missing agents, reassigning misclassified Gaussians, and pruning false positives. To rigorously evaluate this in-the-wild setting, we introduce Wild-30, a challenging benchmark of internet and generative videos. Extensive experiments on KITTI and Wild-30 validate that AGG consistently outperforms state-of-the-art approaches in visual fidelity and robustness under noisy priors.

</details>


### [23] [The GEST-Engine: From Event Graphs to Synthetic Video. A Full Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12231)
*Nicolae Cudlenco,Mihai Masala,Marius Leordeanu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GEST-Engine is a complete system that converts natural-language text into fully-annotated multi-actor video using an explicit world model (Graph of Events in Space and Time), with a four-stage execution pipeline producing dense annotations (RGB, depth, segmentation, pose, bounding boxes, spatial relations, temporal mappings) at zero marginal cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between natural language and high-quality multimodal video data, enabling the generation of richly annotated, temporally consistent multi-actor video from semantic specifications without manual annotation effort.

Method: The system uses an explicit world model (GEST) expressed in a commercial game engine via a multiplayer scripting framework. GESTs are generated procedurally or by an LLM Director with programmatic validation. The pipeline includes: graph parsing/validation, entity/action grounding, temporal orchestration (Allen constraints solved via Floyd-Warshall), and execution/capture.

Result: The engine emits frame-aligned RGB video, per-pixel depth, instance segmentation, per-actor skeletal pose, pairwise spatial-relation graphs, 2D bounding boxes, event-to-frame temporal mappings, and NL descriptions at zero marginal annotation cost. Guarantees object permanence, multi-actor coordination, and temporal consistency by construction.

Conclusion: The system's output serves as valuable training data, evaluation benchmarks, and diagnostic tools for video understanding, with applications in large-scale corpus generation across parallel virtual machines.

Abstract: We present the GEST-Engine, a complete system that goes from natural-language text to fully-annotated multi-actor video. At its core is an explicit world model: rather than encoding state as a learned latent, the engine maintains a complete, inspectable representation of the world (which actors exist, where they are, what they are doing, which objects they hold, and how events relate in time and space), expressed as a formal Graph of Events in Space and Time (GEST) and realized deterministically inside the open world of a commercial game engine driven through an open-source multiplayer scripting framework. GESTs are produced either procedurally or by an agentic text-to-GEST system in which an LLM Director plans a story through tool calls validated by a programmatic state backend, so every generated specification is executable by construction. A GEST then enters a four-stage execution pipeline: graph parsing and validation, entity and action grounding, temporal orchestration (Allen-style constraints resolved by Floyd-Warshall transitive closure), and execution and capture. In a single simulation pass the engine emits frame-aligned RGB video, dense per-pixel depth, instance segmentation, per-actor skeletal pose, per-frame pairwise spatial-relation graphs, 2D bounding boxes, event-to-frame temporal mappings, and natural-language descriptions, all at zero marginal annotation cost. We further describe an in-game world editor, runtime capability extraction, a text-generation pipeline, and a production system that renders corpora at scale across parallel virtual machines. Because every frame traces back to a semantic specification, the engine guarantees object permanence, multi-actor coordination, and temporal consistency by construction, making its output valuable as training data, evaluation benchmarks, and diagnostic tools for video understanding.

</details>


### [24] [Rough Path Signature-Guided Geometry Augmentation for Few-Shot Industrial Surface Defect Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12245)
*Jiaqi Kuang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RPS-GA is a geometry-aware data augmentation for few-shot industrial defect detection that uses second-order path signatures (specifically Lévy-area) from Canny edge contours to highlight boundary structures, achieving significant improvements over a baseline YOLOv8n detector on NEU-DET and PCB-Defect datasets, especially under extremely limited supervision (e.g., 5-10 shots).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard supervised detectors perform poorly on boundary-dominated industrial defects under few-shot settings, motivating the need for a geometry-aware approach that leverages boundary structure without requiring meta-learning or detector redesign.

Method: Canny edge contours are treated as ordered planar paths, and their truncated second-order signature responses (antisymmetric Lévy-area term) are aggregated into a spatial map; two fusion operators (SIG-AUG and SGAA) are designed to highlight boundary-related structure, and the approach is evaluated using an unmodified YOLOv8n detector.

Result: On NEU-DET, SIG-AUG raises 10-shot mAP@0.5 from 0.341 to 0.583; on PCB-Defect, SGAA improves 10-shot mAP@0.5 from 0.086 to 0.299 and enables usable 5-shot detection where baseline fails. Gains diminish as more labeled data becomes available. Multi-seed evaluation confirms trends.

Conclusion: Second-order path-signature geometry provides a practical, detector-agnostic way to strengthen few-shot industrial defect detection without meta-learning or detector redesign.

Abstract: Few-shot industrial defect detection remains difficult for standard supervised detectors, which achieve poor performance on boundary-dominated industrial defects. This paper proposes rough path signature-guided geometry augmentation (RPS-GA), a geometry-aware approach in which Canny edge contours are treated as ordered planar paths whose truncated second-order signature responses, especially the antisymmetric Lévy-area term, are aggregated into a spatial map that highlights boundary-related structure through two fusion operators, SIG-AUG and SGAA. The approach is evaluated on NEU-DET and PCB-Defect under a few-shot protocol with 5, 10, 20, or 50 labeled images per class, using an unmodified YOLOv8n detector throughout. Compared with the baseline, RPS-GA delivers large gains when supervision is limited, although the margin shrinks as more labels become available. On NEU-DET, SIG-AUG raises 10-shot mAP@0.5 from 0.341 to 0.583, whereas on PCB-Defect, SGAA improves 10-shot mAP@0.5 from 0.086 to 0.299 and yields usable detection at 5-shot where the baseline fails entirely. These trends are confirmed by multi-seed evaluation across independent random partitions. Overall, the results indicate that second-order path-signature geometry offers a practical way to strengthen few-shot industrial defect detection without meta-learning or detector redesign.

</details>


### [25] [How to Realize Recursively Self-Improving Agents and Personal Singularity: A Goal-, Scope-, Tool-, and Benchmark-Driven Multi-Agent Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12254)
*Chengshuai Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a governed multi-agent architecture for recursively self-improving LLM agents, with personal singularity as a bounded human-AI co-development objective—helping a user approach an expanding, user-defined capability frontier across selected domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address two linked questions: how an agent can improve its own learning and acting mechanisms, and how that improvement can durably increase user capabilities rather than just software capabilities.

Method: A governed multi-agent architecture with goal contracts, bounded scopes, validated tool registries, tool-level tests, end-to-end benchmarks, autonomy/routing policies, memory, and improvement policies. Includes a fast planner-executor-verifier loop, slower evidence-gated improvement loop, external governance plane, decentralized agent lineages, owner-directed agent foundry, and Personal Singularity OS coordinating working, computational-imaging, process-learning, and personal-learning agents.

Result: Formalization of scope, routing, improvement acceptance, bounded goal evolution, tool-first execution, and human capability transfer. Provides safety invariants, benchmark design, and an implementation roadmap.

Conclusion: A position and systems-design paper; unrestricted recursive self-improvement or personal singularity has not yet been achieved, but the architecture provides a framework toward these goals with safety considerations.

Abstract: Large language model (LLM) agents can increasingly plan, use tools, maintain memory, and execute long-horizon tasks. These advances motivate two linked questions: how can an agent improve the mechanisms by which it learns and acts, and how can that improvement increase the durable capabilities of its user rather than only the software itself? This paper proposes a governed multi-agent architecture for recursively self-improving agents and introduces personal singularity as a bounded human-AI co-development objective: helping a user approach an expanding, user-defined capability frontier across selected domains. Each agent is defined by a goal contract, bounded scope, validated tool registry, tool-level tests, end-to-end benchmarks, an owner-controlled autonomy policy, a routing policy, memory, and an improvement policy. Out-of-scope tasks are transferred to another accountable agent or to a newly created niche agent. A user-facing Auto-Index selects interactive, hybrid, autonomous, or scheduled operation without overriding external permissions. The architecture combines a fast planner-executor-verifier loop, a slower evidence-gated improvement loop, an external governance plane, decentralized agent lineages, an owner-directed agent foundry, and a Personal Singularity OS coordinating working, computational-imaging, process-learning, and personal-learning agents. We formalize scope, routing, improvement acceptance, bounded goal evolution, tool-first execution, and human capability transfer, and provide safety invariants, benchmark design, and an implementation roadmap. This is a position and systems-design paper, not evidence that unrestricted recursive self-improvement or personal singularity has already been achieved.

</details>


### [26] [Auditing Data Leakage in Whole-Slide Image Multimodal Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12278)
*Wenhao Zhang,Zhongliang Zhou,John Kang,Sheng Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper uncovers that reported zero-shot performance of vision-language models on whole-slide image VQA benchmarks is severely inflated due to patient-level and institutional-level data leakage, with train-test overlaps of 92.3–100% on TCGA-derived benchmarks and near-complete TSS overlap, and proposes recommendations for contamination-free evaluation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To audit and expose the root cause of suspiciously high zero-shot performance claimed by recent VLMs on WSI VQA benchmarks, hypothesizing that data leakage rather than genuine multimodal reasoning drives these results.

Method: The authors trace canonical identifiers (slide, case, TSS) across public resources such as TCGA; measure case-level and TSS-level train-test overlap; analyze whether leakage is linearly decodable from foundation-model feature spaces; and quantify accuracy gaps between leaked and clean cases across multiple published WSI VLMs.

Result: Case-level train-test overlaps of 92.3–100% on TCGA-derived benchmarks and near-complete TSS overlap were found; leakage is linearly decodable from foundation-model features; a measurable accuracy gap exists between leaked and audit-clean cases; peak reported accuracies concentrate on the most contaminated benchmarks.

Conclusion: Current WSI VQA evaluation is fundamentally compromised and cannot distinguish genuine multimodal reasoning from nearest-neighbor retrieval over memorized artifacts; the paper provides concrete recommendations for constructing contamination-free benchmarks, provenance disclosure, and automated overlap auditing.

Abstract: Recent vision-language models (VLMs) for computational pathology report striking zero-shot performance on whole-slide image (WSI) visual question answering (VQA) benchmarks. We audit these claims and find them fundamentally compromised by data leakage at two hierarchical levels: patient-level leakage, where slides from the same case appear in both training and test folds, and institutional-level leakage, where different cases nonetheless share staining-batch and scanner signatures through a common Tissue Source Site (TSS). By tracing canonical slide, case, and TSS identifiers across major public resources, we document case level train test overlaps of 92.3~100% on TCGA-derived benchmarks, together with near-complete TSS overlap. We further demonstrate that both leakage levels are linearly decodable from foundation-model feature space, that they induce a measurable accuracy gap between leaked and audit-clean cases on a published checkpoint, and that across multiple published WSI VLMs, peak reported accuracies concentrate on the most heavily contaminated benchmarks. Therefore, the current WSI VQA evaluation cannot distinguish genuine multimodal reasoning from nearest-neighbor retrieval over memorized institutional and patient-specific artifacts. Finally, we outline concrete recommendations for contamination-free evaluation.
