Abstract: When labeled data are scarce, off-the-shelf diffusion models can augment training sets for few-shot medical image classification, but not all generated samples are equally useful for the downstream task. Existing approaches largely improve synthetic data by increasing realism, diversity, or domain adaptation, while overlooking a more fundamental question: how should sample usefulness for classification be measured and optimized? We address this with Class-Contrastive Influence (C2I), a criterion that quantifies a sample's usefulness through its gradient-based influence on the classifier. We find that effective samples exhibit a strong C2I gap: their loss gradients align with validation gradients from the same class and oppose those from other classes. Our analysis further suggests that such high-C2I samples are hard, boundary-proximal examples that help refine the decision boundary and improve robustness. Building on this insight, we fine-tune diffusion models with reinforcement learning using a C2I-based reward to steer generation toward class-informative samples. Across several few-shot medical imaging benchmarks, C2I-guided generation improves downstream accuracy and robustness over diffusion-based augmentation baselines, showing that synthetic augmentation is most effective when guided by task usefulness rather than image quality alone.

</details>


### [52] [Self in Space: Benchmarking Self-Awareness and Spatial Cognition in UAV Embodied Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12477)
*Zhishan Zou,Guoyan Sun,Zhiwei Wei,Jiancheng Pan,Yujie Li,Mugen Peng,Wenjia Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SIS-Bench is a benchmark evaluating embodied spatial intelligence in UAVs through a unified self-in-space formulation, revealing MLLMs' limitations in self-awareness and dynamic modeling, and showing that motion-aware representations improve performance across cognitive levels and downstream tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing UAV benchmarks are environment-centric and neglect agent self-awareness; the paper aims to address this gap by introducing a benchmark that jointly evaluates spatial cognition and self-awareness in embodied UAV scenarios.

Method: SIS-Bench organizes evaluation along space and self dimensions with a three-level hierarchy (perception, memory, reasoning), containing 4,856 QA pairs across 13 tasks from 1,646 real-world UAV videos via a task-conditioned construction pipeline with expert verification. A motion-aware representation using optical flow and visual feature fusion is also explored.

Result: Current MLLMs show fundamental limitations in dynamic/agent-centered processes, an imbalance between spatial cognition and self-awareness, and progressive degradation across cognitive levels. Motion-aware representation consistently improves perception and memory for both spatial cognition and self-awareness, and generalizes to downstream UAV decision-making.

Conclusion: Self-awareness is critical for advancing embodied spatial intelligence in UAVs; the paper provides SIS-Bench as a standardized benchmark and empirical evidence supporting motion-aware self-in-space modeling.

Abstract: Autonomous UAV systems increasingly rely on multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to operate in complex real-world environments. Such embodied scenarios require not only understanding the surrounding space but also maintaining a coherent representation of the agent itself. However, existing UAV-oriented approaches and benchmarks remain largely environment-centric, primarily focusing on spatial understanding tasks, with the agent's self-awareness remaining implicit. To address this gap, we introduce SIS-Bench, a benchmark for evaluating embodied spatial intelligence in UAV scenarios under a unified self-in-space formulation. SIS-Bench organizes evaluation along two complementary dimensions, space and self, and a three-level hierarchy of perception, memory, and reasoning. It contains 4,856 question--answer pairs across 13 tasks derived from 1,646 real-world UAV videos through a task-conditioned construction pipeline with expert verification.Extensive evaluations reveal that current MLLMs exhibit fundamental limitations in modeling dynamic and agent-centered processes. In particular, we observe a clear imbalance between spatial cognition and self-awareness, as well as a progressive performance degradation across cognitive levels.Motivated by these findings, we further explore a motion-aware representation that incorporates self-related dynamics through optical flow and visual feature fusion. Experimental results show that modeling agent motion consistently improves perception and memory performance, not only in spatial cognition but also in self-awareness, and generalizes to downstream UAV decision-making tasks.Our results highlight the importance of self-awareness for advancing embodied spatial intelligence, and provide both a new benchmark and empirical evidence for motion-aware self-in-space modeling.

</details>


### [53] [RealSkin: Spatio-Spectral Partial Neural Adjoint Maps for Image-to-3D Attribute Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12495)
*Jing Li,Yawei Luo,Xiangze Meng,Ying Li,Tieru Wu,Rui Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RealSkin is a self-supervised framework that transfers photorealistic visual attributes from real images to synthetic 3D surfaces by combining spatial-guided registration with a spectral-aware neural adjoint network, achieving state-of-the-art performance despite topological discrepancies and non-isometric deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods for transferring visual attributes from real observations to synthetic 3D surfaces struggle with resolution mismatch, discrete point correspondences, near-isometry constraints, and topological inconsistencies. The goal is to bridge the appearance gap between real-world and synthetic assets without these limitations.

Method: RealSkin uses a self-supervised framework with two main components: (1) a spatial-guided registration algorithm that establishes coarse correspondences under severe topological discrepancies, and (2) a spectral-aware neural adjoint network that incorporates partial correspondences into a neural function space and models non-isometric residuals for correspondence refinement in a learned spectral domain.

Result: The method achieves state-of-the-art performance on challenging real-to-synthetic scenarios.

Conclusion: RealSkin effectively addresses the limitations of prior work by relaxing strict isometric assumptions and handling partial correspondences, enabling robust real-to-synthetic attribute transfer for photorealistic 3D asset creation.

Abstract: Creating photorealistic 3D assets requires bridging the appearance gap between real-world observations and synthetic models. A promising approach is to transfer visual attributes from real images onto synthetic 3D surfaces. Traditional methods struggle with resolution mismatch and the inherent discreteness of point correspondences. In contrast, resolution-robust functional maps enable smooth attribute propagation but rely on near-isometry assumptions and topological consistency. To address these limitations, we propose RealSkin, a self-supervised framework that performs correspondence optimization in a learned spectral domain, guided by spatial correspondences. We first introduce a spatial-guided registration algorithm to establish coarse correspondences under severe topological discrepancies. To relax strict isometric assumptions and handle partial correspondences, we further design a spectral-aware neural adjoint network that incorporates partial correspondences into a neural function space and models non-isometric residuals for correspondence refinement. Experimental results demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance on challenging real-to-synthetic scenarios. The code will be publicly released.

</details>


### [54] [TerraLogic: A Benchmark for Hierarchical Geospatial Reasoning in Earth Observation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12497)
*Yuhang Yan,Linchao Mou,Bokang Yang,Qingyu Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TerraLogic is a benchmark for geospatial reasoning with 545 hierarchy-aware tasks across optical, SAR, and IR imagery, and HieraPlan is a tool-augmented agent with fault-tolerant hierarchical reasoning that outperforms current approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing remote sensing studies focus on perception-oriented tasks, leaving cognitive geospatial reasoning (e.g., hazard assessment, urban heat island analysis) largely underexplored.

Method: TerraLogic: 545 scenario-driven, hierarchy-aware tasks spanning optical, SAR, and IR imagery. HieraPlan: a tool-augmented agent that organizes toolkits into functional hierarchies with fault-tolerant reasoning, structured abstraction, robust recovery from tool failures, and stable long-horizon planning.

Result: Current approaches struggle with hierarchical geospatial reasoning; HieraPlan provides a strong baseline with improved reasoning, cross-modal generalization, and error handling.

Conclusion: The work bridges perception and cognition in remote sensing by introducing benchmarks and agents for hierarchical geospatial reasoning, with public dataset and code available.

Abstract: Beyond perception, reasoning is essential in remote sensing for advanced interpretation, inference, and decision-making. Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have enabled tool-augmented agents that leverage external tools to perform complex analytical tasks. However, existing studies in remote sensing primarily focus on perception-oriented tasks, leaving cognitive geospatial reasoning largely underexplored. To address this gap, we introduce TerraLogic, a benchmark for geospatial reasoning. TerraLogic comprises 545 scenario-driven, hierarchy-aware tasks, such as hazard vulnerability assessment, urban heat island analysis, and forest fragmentation dynamics, spanning optical, Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR), and infrared (IR) imagery. It advances evaluation beyond recognition and monitoring toward cognitive-level geospatial analysis. To facilitate evaluation on TerraLogic, we further propose HieraPlan, a tool-augmented agent that organizes toolkits into functional hierarchies and performs fault-tolerant reasoning. HieraPlan enables structured abstraction, robust recovery from tool failures, and stable long-horizon planning. Extensive experiments demonstrate that current approaches struggle with hierarchical geospatial reasoning, while HieraPlan provides a strong baseline with improved reasoning, cross-modal generalization, and error handling. The dataset and agent code are publicly available at https://github.com/Ireliya/TerraLogic.

</details>


### [55] [DynTrace: Tracking Dynamic Object Evidence for 4D Spatio-Temporal Reasoning in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12503)
*Rongxin Gao,Yuzhi Huang,Dongxuan Liu,Chu Li,Zhenye Wang,Jie Wu,Shuzhao Xie,Jingyan Jiang,Xinghao Ding,Xiaotong Tu,Yue Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DynTrace is a training-free framework that improves 4D spatio-temporal reasoning in MLLMs by tracking dynamic object evidence through Dynamic Trajectory Visualization and Dynamic Trace Tokens, achieving SOTA results on multiple benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current MLLMs struggle with continuous dynamic scene perception in 4D spatio-temporal reasoning due to reliance on sparse frame-level observations, which fragment dynamic cues and fail to disentangle genuine object motion from camera-induced apparent motion.

Method: DynTrace introduces two components: (1) Dynamic Trajectory Visualization (DTV) reprojects world-coordinate trajectories onto the image plane to provide geometry-informed visual priors; (2) Dynamic Trace Token (DT-Token) organized into a Dynamic Trace Graph (DTG) tracks object-level dynamic cues, trace evolution, and key moments for continuous evidence maintenance.

Result: DynTrace consistently improves open-source MLLMs and achieves state-of-the-art results on Dyn-Bench, VLM4D, and DSI-Bench.

Conclusion: Tracking dynamic object evidence grounded in geometry-informed visual priors and structured spatio-temporal traces is crucial for robust 4D spatio-temporal reasoning.

Abstract: 4D spatio-temporal reasoning, jointly modeling 3D spatial structure and temporal evolution, is essential for understanding dynamic worlds and enabling embodied interaction. While current Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) show strong capabilities in static scene understanding and coarse-grained 4D tasks, they still have notable limitations in continuous dynamic scene perception, especially in tracking dynamic object evidence for coherent 4D spatio-temporal reasoning. This shortcoming stems mainly from relying on sparse frame-level observations, fragmenting continuous dynamic cues and leaving models unable to disentangle genuine object dynamics from camera-induced apparent motion. Inspired by humans tracking dynamic cues while compensating for viewpoint changes, we propose DynTrace, a training-free framework for 4D spatio-temporal reasoning with two complementary components. Dynamic Trajectory Visualization (DTV) reprojects world-coordinate trajectories onto the image plane, providing geometry-informed visual priors that disentangle genuine object dynamics from camera-induced apparent motion. Meanwhile, the Dynamic Trace Token (DT-Token), organized into a Dynamic Trace Graph (DTG), tracks object-level dynamic cues, trace evolution, and key moments, maintaining continuous dynamic object evidence for coherent 4D reasoning. Together, these two components equip MLLMs with continuously tracked dynamic object evidence, grounded in geometry-informed visual priors and structured spatio-temporal traces. DynTrace consistently improves open-source MLLMs, achieving state-of-the-art results on Dyn-Bench, VLM4D, and DSI-Bench, validating the importance of tracking dynamic object evidence for robust 4D spatio-temporal reasoning.

</details>


### [56] [DiTailed: Ensuring Visual Object Consistency in Text-Image-to-Image Flow Matching Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12539)
*Francesco Taioli,Daniel Coelho,Iaroslav Melekhov,Roberto Alcover-Couso,Jose Miguel Grande Saiz,Virginia Fernandez Arguedas,Artur Bekasov*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper addresses preserving visual object consistency in text-guided image editing by introducing ABO-Edit (a 12,000+ triplet dataset), discovering that the conditioning embedding space in rectified flow models predicts final images even at high noise levels, and proposing FlowMirror (a parameter-free auxiliary loss) that improves generation quality without architectural changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Generative models for text-guided image editing frequently fail to preserve visual object consistency (i.e., maintaining a subject's key attributes throughout editing), and there is a lack of dedicated datasets and methods to study and address this limitation.

Method: (1) ABO-Edit dataset: 12,000+ triplets of source images, editing prompts, and high-quality target images from 3D assets with multi-view coverage and human verification. (2) Discovery that the conditioning embedding space in rectified flow models encodes predictions of the final image even at high noise levels. (3) FlowMirror: a parameter-free auxiliary loss that supervises this conditioning embedding space.

Result: FlowMirror improves generation quality across several metrics over baselines, without requiring architectural changes.

Conclusion: Exploiting the overlooked property of the conditioning embedding space in rectified flow models enables effective object consistency preservation in image editing via a simple, parameter-free loss.

Abstract: Despite remarkable progress in text-guided image editing, generative models frequently fail to preserve visual object consistency, defined as the preservation of a subject's key attributes throughout the editing process. We address this limitation through three contributions. First, we introduce ABO-Edit, a dataset specifically designed to study object consistency, comprising over 12,000 triplets of source images, editing prompts, and high-quality target images rendered from artist-designed 3D assets, with multi-view coverage and human-verified quality control. Second, we uncover an overlooked property of image-editing rectified flow models: the conditioning embedding space, not directly supervised during training, encodes a prediction of the final generated image even at high noise levels. Third, exploiting this finding, we propose FlowMirror, a parameter-free auxiliary loss that supervises this conditioning embedding space. Without architectural changes, our method improves generation quality across several metrics over baselines.

</details>


### [57] [Edge-Aware Thermal Infrared UAV Swarm Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12544)
*Yu-Hsi Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes an Adaptive Kinematic Kalman Filter (AKKF) for edge-real-time UAV tracking in thermal infrared imaging, balancing robustness and computational efficiency by augmenting linear KF with state-dependent kinematics, transient false-positive suppression, and predictive coasting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing TIR UAV tracking methods prioritize accuracy but overlook computational constraints of real-time edge deployment. Standard Kalman Filter is efficient but fails under highly dynamic UAV motion and thermal sensor jitter, while nonlinear estimators improve robustness at higher computational cost.

Method: Proposes an edge-aware online tracking pipeline centered on AKKF, which augments linear KF with state-dependent kinematic modeling. Combined with transient false-positive suppression and kinematics-driven predictive coasting to improve trajectory continuity.

Result: Experiments on the Beyond Strong Baseline (BSB) benchmark jointly evaluate tracking performance and computational efficiency, demonstrating improved trajectory continuity under challenging TIR conditions.

Conclusion: AKKF offers a practical trade-off between robustness and real-time efficiency for edge-deployed TIR UAV tracking, providing a starting point for future edge-aware tracking solutions.

Abstract: Thermal infrared (TIR) imaging is essential for UAV swarm operations in visually degraded environments. However, tracking tiny UAVs remains challenging due to limited appearance cues, frequent occlusions, and rapid maneuvers. Despite significant progress driven by benchmarks such as the Anti-UAV challenge, existing methods primarily prioritize accuracy while overlooking the computational constraints of real-time edge deployment. The standard Kalman Filter (KF) offers the efficiency required for edge devices, yet its constant-velocity assumption often breaks down under highly dynamic UAV motion and thermal sensor jitter. More sophisticated nonlinear estimators can improve robustness but often introduce additional computational costs. To address this gap, we propose an edge-aware online tracking pipeline centered on the Adaptive Kinematic Kalman Filter (AKKF), which augments the linear KF with state-dependent kinematic modeling while preserving real-time efficiency. Combined with transient false-positive suppression and kinematics-driven predictive coasting, the presented pipeline improves trajectory continuity under challenging TIR conditions. Experiments on the Beyond Strong Baseline (BSB) benchmark provide a starting point for edge-aware UAV tracking by jointly evaluating tracking performance and computational efficiency, offering insights toward future real-time deployment.

</details>


### [58] [CGRL: Concept-Guided Pruning and Representation Learning for Whole-Slide Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12556)
*Thuc Huynh,Tuan Le,Doanh C. Bui*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CGRL introduces class-level concept prototypes derived from disease prompts into MIL for WSI classification, using concept-relevance pruning to select top-K relevant patches and concept-guided contrastive learning to regularize representations, improving accuracy and macro-F1 while reducing computational cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing MIL methods for weakly supervised WSI classification aggregate patch embeddings using mainly visual cues, retaining many non-informative patches and providing weak alignment between instance features and class-level disease semantics.

Method: (1) Concept-relevance pruning: ranks patches by similarity to class concept prototypes and retains top-K relevant patches. (2) Concept-guided contrastive representation learning: constructs class-wise positive/negative patch sets from the similarity matrix and optimizes target-class, symmetric auxiliary, and cross-class separation objectives.

Result: CGRL improves multiple model-dataset combinations on TCGA-BRCA and TCGA-NSCLC, achieving clear improvements in accuracy and macro-F1 while reducing computational cost through pruning.

Conclusion: Class-level semantic concepts provide an effective and practical prior for patch selection and representation learning in weakly supervised computational pathology.

Abstract: Weakly supervised whole-slide image (WSI) classification is widely used in computational pathology because slide-level labels are easier to obtain than dense region annotations. Existing multiple instance learning (MIL) methods often aggregate large bags of patch embeddings using mainly visual cues, which can retain many non-informative patches and provide weak alignment between instance features and class-level disease semantics. We propose Concept-Guided Pruning and Representation Learning (CGRL), a simple framework that introduces class-level concept prototypes derived from disease prompts into the MIL pipeline. First, concept-relevance pruning ranks patch instances by their similarity to class concepts and retains the top-K concept-relevant patches for downstream MIL aggregation. Second, concept-guided contrastive representation learning constructs class-wise positive and negative patch sets from the same similarity matrix and optimizes target-class, symmetric auxiliary, and cross-class separation objectives, thereby regularizing the projected concept space. We evaluate CGRL on TCGA-BRCA and TCGA-NSCLC using multiple representative MIL methods. Experimental results show that CGRL improves several model-dataset combinations, with gains depending on the downstream MIL model and dataset. It achieves particularly clear improvements in accuracy and macro-F1 while reducing computational cost through concept-relevance pruning. These findings demonstrate that class-level semantic concepts provide an effective and practical prior for patch selection and representation learning in weakly supervised computational pathology.

</details>


### [59] [Gaussian Mixture Modeling for Event-Aware Visual Allocation in Long Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12557)
*Yifan Lu,Ziqi Zhang,Chunfeng Yuan,Jun Gao,Bing Li,Weiming Hu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GMM-EVA is a training-free, plug-and-play framework that uses Gaussian Mixture Models to model event-level structures in long videos, enabling differentiated visual allocation (high-res keyframes per event + low-res secondary frames) to reduce token budget by ~50% while maintaining or improving performance on long video understanding tasks compared to uniform sampling and baseline methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LVLMs suffer from high computational cost and information loss in long video understanding due to uniform sampling, and existing keyframe selection methods treat frames as atomic entities with equal visual budgets, overlooking high-level semantic structures and introducing redundancy.

Method: GMM-EVA applies Gaussian Mixture Models to model event-level structure from discrete frame-wise observations, then uses a differentiated allocation strategy: one primary high-resolution keyframe per event for detail, plus lower-resolution secondary keyframes for temporal context, optimizing token budgets.

Result: GMM-EVA significantly outperforms uniform sampling across multiple long video benchmarks, and achieves comparable performance to baseline selection methods while using only about half the visual token budget.

Conclusion: GMM-EVA is an efficient, training-free, plug-and-play framework that generalizes robustly across various relevance measures and downstream LVLMs, substantially improving efficiency-effectiveness trade-off in long video understanding.

Abstract: Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) face significant challenges in long video understanding due to the excessive computational cost and information loss associated with uniform sampling. Existing keyframe selection methods often treat video frames as atomic entities and allocate visual budgets equally, thereby overlooking high-level semantic structures and introducing substantial redundancy. To address these limitations, we propose GMM-EVA (Gaussian Mixture Modeling for Event-Aware Visual Allocation), which leverages Gaussian Mixture Models to model event-level structure from discrete frame-wise observations. A differentiated allocation strategy is then applied to preserve one primary high-resolution keyframe per event for high-fidelity detail, while utilizing lower-resolution secondary keyframes to maintain temporal context and optimize token budgets. GMM-EVA is a training-free, plug-and-play framework that generalizes robustly across various relevance measures and downstream LVLMs. Extensive experiments on multiple long video benchmarks demonstrate that our method significantly outperforms uniform sampling. Notably, GMM-EVA achieves comparable performance to baseline selection methods while utilizing only approximately half of the visual token budget, highlighting its superior efficiency and effectiveness.

</details>


### [60] [WanToFight: Real-Time Generative Game Engine for Multi-Player Combat Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12592)
*Li Hu,Guangyuan Wang,Peng Zhang,Bang Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: WanToFight is a generative game engine that simulates real-time two-player KOF '97 gameplay from keyboard input, using a video diffusion transformer with autoregressive generation, player association, and keyboard injection modules. It achieves 30FPS at 512x384 on a single RTX 5090, combining multi-player control, real-time inference, complex physical interaction, and adversarial gameplay for the first time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior generative game engines only support single-player first-person or non-real-time cooperative scenarios; multi-player control, real-time inference, complex physical interaction, and adversarial gameplay have not been jointly addressed.

Method: Three components built on Wan-1.3B video diffusion transformer: (1) streaming autoregressive generator with block-causal attention and rolling KV cache; (2) visually grounded Player Association module binding keyboard signals to character identities; (3) gated, locally causal keyboard injection module trained with single-player-to-full-gameplay curriculum. Uses four-step DMD-distilled student with pruned VAE decoder.

Result: Sustains 30FPS at 512x384 on a single NVIDIA RTX 5090 over a complete match. First generative game engine to combine multi-player control, real-time inference, complex physical interaction, and adversarial gameplay.

Conclusion: WanToFight successfully demonstrates that a generative game engine can handle real-time multi-player adversarial gameplay with complex physical interactions, closing a significant gap in prior work.

Abstract: We present WanToFight, a generative game engine that simulates real-time, two-player The King of Fighters '97 (KOF~'97) gameplay from keyboard input. Prior generative game engines target either single-player first-person settings or non-real-time cooperative scenarios; multi-player control, real-time inference, complex physical interaction, and adversarial gameplay have not been jointly addressed. WanToFight closes this gap with three components built on the Wan-1.3B video diffusion transformer: a streaming autoregressive generator with block-causal attention and a rolling KV cache; a visually grounded Player Association module that binds each player's keyboard signal to a character identity; and a gated, locally causal keyboard injection module trained with a single-player-to-full-gameplay curriculum. A four-step DMD-distilled student paired with a pruned VAE decoder sustains 30FPS at 512x384 on a single NVIDIA RTX 5090 over the duration of a complete match. To our knowledge, WanToFight is the first generative game engine to combine multi-player control, real-time inference, complex physical interaction, and adversarial gameplay in one system.

</details>


### [61] [Decouple and Reason: Anatomically Guided Two-Stage Voxel-Level Grounding of Free-Text Findings in 3D Chest CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12602)
*Kwang-Hyun Uhm,Inhwa Son,Sung-Jea Ko*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes a decoupled framework (DAGG) for voxel-level grounding of free-text findings in 3D chest CT, splitting the task into class-agnostic lesion segmentation and text-volume reasoning with anatomical guidance, achieving SOTA on ReXGroundingCT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Automatic voxel-level grounding of free-text findings in 3D chest CT is critical for clinical interpretability but remains challenging due to spatial complexity of large 3D volumes and heterogeneity of free-text findings. Existing end-to-end approaches struggle to simultaneously learn localized feature representations for segmentation and semantic understanding for text alignment.

Method: A decoupled framework with two specialized stages: (1) class-agnostic lesion segmentation to extract candidate sub-volumes, and (2) text-volume reasoning augmented with explicit anatomical guidance (relative spatial coordinates and lung lobe priors) for cross-modal alignment.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance in overall grounding quality on the ReXGroundingCT benchmark leaderboard.

Conclusion: Decoupling detection from reasoning is a highly effective paradigm for handling the complexity of 3D medical visual grounding.

Abstract: Automatic voxel-level grounding of free-text findings in 3D chest Computed Tomography (CT) is critical for clinical interpretability. However, this task remains highly challenging due to the intricate spatial complexity of large 3D volumes and the heterogeneity of free-text findings. Existing end-to-end approaches often struggle to simultaneously learn the localized feature representations required for accurate 3D segmentation and the complex semantic understanding needed for text alignment, leading to suboptimal grounding performance. To overcome this fundamental limitation, we propose a novel decoupled framework that disentangles the problem into two specialized stages: (1) class-agnostic lesion segmentation and (2) text-volume reasoning. This structural separation allows the model to first extract candidate sub-volumes by localizing potential abnormalities. Subsequently, intensive cross-modal reasoning is performed to align these localized sub-volumes with free-text medical findings. To resolve the spatial ambiguities inherent in local regions, the reasoning module is augmented with explicit anatomical guidance, utilizing relative spatial coordinates and lung lobe priors. Evaluated on the ReXGroundingCT benchmark, our method achieves state-of-the-art performance in overall grounding quality on the official leaderboard. These results demonstrate that decoupling detection from reasoning is a highly effective paradigm for handling the complexity of 3D medical visual grounding. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/khuhm/DAGG.

</details>


### [62] [Towards Vision-Free CIR: Attribute-Augmented Scoring and LLM-Based Reranking for Zero-Shot Composed Image Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12621)
*Ryotaro Shimada,Yu-Chieh Lin,Yuji Nozawa,Youyang Ng,Osamu Torii,Yusuke Matsui*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a Vision-Free Composed Image Retrieval (CIR) framework that uses Attribute-Augmented Hybrid Scoring and LLM-Based Reranking to overcome information loss from representing images as text, achieving state-of-the-art zero-shot performance on CIRR (44.04% R@1, +8.79%) while revealing trade-offs on FashionIQ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether Vision-Free approaches (representing images as text) can effectively handle Composed Image Retrieval (CIR)—a complex multimodal task—given the inherent information loss in textual descriptions, and to address the limitations of this paradigm.

Method: (1) Attribute-Augmented Hybrid Scoring: compensates for lost visual details via explicit attribute matching; (2) LLM-Based Reranking: verifies semantic consistency of top candidates.

Result: Outperforms existing Zero-shot CIR methods on CIRR (44.04% R@1, +8.79%); on FashionIQ, reveals a trade-off between semantic reasoning and fine-grained visual matching. Ablation studies confirm both components consistently improve performance.

Conclusion: Vision-Free approaches can be effective for CIR with appropriate compensatory techniques, but fine-grained visual matching remains challenging, particularly in domain-specific settings like fashion.

Abstract: Recent work has shown that "Vision-Free'' approaches (representing images as text) can be effective for standard image retrieval tasks. However, it remains unclear whether this paradigm can effectively handle a more complex, multimodal task, Composed Image Retrieval (CIR), due to the inherent information loss in textual descriptions. In this paper, we introduce a Vision-Free CIR framework that addresses this challenge through two key techniques: (1) Attribute-Augmented Hybrid Scoring, which compensates for lost visual details via explicit attribute matching, and (2) LLM-Based Reranking, which verifies semantic consistency of top candidates. Experiments on the open-domain CIRR dataset show that our approach outperforms existing Zero-shot CIR methods (44.04% R@1, +8.79%). On FashionIQ, our results highlight the trade-off between semantic reasoning and fine-grained visual matching. Ablation studies reveal that both attribute-augmented scoring and LLM-Based Reranking consistently improve performance.

</details>


### [63] [MAGE: Color-Invariant and Spatial Knowledge Distillation for Gastric Neoplasm Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12663)
*Jiho Jun,Jeongwon Woo,Jaemin Song,Thanh Bong Nguyen,Dong-heon Yeon,Donghoon Kang,Jae-Myung Park,Sung-Jea Ko,Kwang-Hyun Uhm*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MAGE is a framework that uses masked achromatic views and dual-objective distillation to train a model for gastric adenoma vs. carcinoma classification, forcing morphology-based predictions and outperforming detection/classification baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Endoscopic differentiation between gastric adenoma and carcinoma is critical yet challenging due to high inter-class similarity and ambiguous boundaries. Existing ROI-based methods suffer from error propagation and loss of global context, while full-image classification lacks spatial focus. DNNs also exploit texture biases (e.g., bleeding, lighting artifacts) instead of intrinsic morphological features.

Method: A dual-branch framework is proposed: (1) an auxiliary local expert branch trained on masked achromatic views to learn purely structural features by suppressing color and background context; (2) a main branch receiving full white-light imaging (WLI) input, supervised via dual-objective distillation (classification logits + spatial attention maps) from the expert branch, enabling morphology-grounded predictions while retaining color cues.

Result: On a clinical gastric endoscopy dataset, MAGE significantly outperforms detection-based methods (e.g., YOLO) and classification-based methods (e.g., Swin-Transformer), achieving superior classification performance and providing interpretable attention maps for clinical reliability.

Conclusion: The proposed framework effectively forces morphology-grounded predictions, avoids shortcut learning, and delivers real-time deployable models without requiring annotated masks at inference.

Abstract: Accurate differentiation between gastric adenoma and carcinoma during endoscopy is critical for clinical decision-making. Yet, this task is highly challenging due to high inter-class similarity and ambiguous boundaries between the two classes. Existing ROI-based classification methods often suffer from detection/segmentation error propagation and loss of surrounding global context. In contrast, full-image classification lacks the necessary spatial focus. Furthermore, we observe that deep neural networks gravitate towards domain-specific texture biases(e.g. bleeding, lighting artifacts), often causing models to predict based on spurious correlations instead of intrinsic morphological features. To address these limitations, we propose a novel framework, Masked Achromatic Guidance Expert (MAGE). During training, we introduce an auxiliary local expert branch trained on masked achromatic views of the neoplasm. By suppressing background context and color, this branch is forced to learn highly discriminative, purely structural features. We then employ a dual-objective distillation strategy, transferring both classification logits and spatial attention maps to provide implicit spatial supervision to the main branch that receives full WLI as input. This dual-objective distillation forces the model to ground its predictions in morphology rather than relying on shortcuts, while still retaining clinically relevant color cues. At inference time, our deployable model operates on images without annotated masks, ensuring real-time deployability . Extensive experiments on a clinical gastric endoscopy dataset show that our method significantly outperforms existing detection-based methodologies (e.g. YOLO) and classification-based methodologies (e.g. Swin-Transformer), providing not only superior classification performance but also interpretable attention maps for clinical reliability.

</details>


### [64] [Text-Aided Multi-Modal Panoptic Symbol Spotting for CAD Floor Plan Drawings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12678)
*Yan Gong,Bohao Li,Bowen Du,Junchen Ye*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TextCAD is a multimodal framework for panoptic symbol spotting in CAD floor plans that jointly models graphical primitives and textual annotations. It introduces a Type-Attribute Correlation Encoder (TACE) for compositional semantics and a Semantic Hierarchy Alignment framework with Multi-level Semantic Filtering (MSF) for cross-modal semantic fusion, achieving state-of-the-art results on building-design datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing CAD symbol spotting methods are primitive-centric and underexploit textual annotations, and even text-aware approaches fail to model complex syntax and hierarchical semantics of annotations, leading to semantic loss and suboptimal performance.

Method: The proposed TextCAD framework includes (1) a Type-Attribute Correlation Encoder (TACE) that explicitly encodes compositional semantics by jointly modeling annotation types and attributes, and (2) a Semantic Hierarchy Alignment framework with Multi-level Semantic Filtering (MSF) and primitive downsampling that adaptively aligns annotation semantics with graphical primitives at different semantic levels for accurate cross-modal injection and fusion.

Result: Experiments on real-world building-design datasets show that TextCAD effectively improves symbol spotting performance and achieves state-of-the-art results.

Conclusion: Jointly modeling graphical primitives and textual annotations with explicit handling of compositional and hierarchical semantics significantly improves panoptic symbol spotting in CAD floor plans.

Abstract: Computer-Aided Design (CAD) floor plan drawings contain both graphical primitives and textual annotations, which provide complementary geometric and semantic cues for intelligent design understanding. Among CAD analysis tasks, panoptic symbol spotting has become increasingly important with the growing demand for industrial digitalization and deep learning-based automation. However, most existing methods remain primarily primitive-centric and underexploit textual annotations, despite their critical semantic value. Even the few text-aware approaches often treat annotations only superficially, without properly modeling complex syntax and hierarchical semantics of CAD annotations, which leads to semantic loss and suboptimal spotting performance. To address these limitations, we propose TextCAD, a multimodal framework that jointly models graphical primitives and textual annotations for panoptic symbol spotting. Specifically, we design a Type-Attribute Correlation Encoder (TACE) to explicitly encode the compositional semantics within annotations by jointly modeling their types and attributes. We further introduce a Semantic Hierarchy Alignment framework with Multi-level Semantic Filtering (MSF) and primitive downsampling, which adaptively aligns annotation semantics with graphical primitives at different semantic levels and enables accurate cross-modal semantic injection and fusion. Experiments on real-world building-design datasets show that TextCAD effectively improves symbol spotting performance and achieves state-of-the-art results.

</details>


### [65] [ReflectVLN: Training Vision-Language Navigation Agents with Reflective Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12680)
*Jiahang Wang,Yirong Yang,Yanqing Zhu,Minghua Luo,Shichao Xie,Fei Liu,Mu Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ReflectVLN is an agentic vision-language navigation framework with bidirectionally interactive intention and execution agents that enables closed-loop error recovery, subtask reflection, and interpretable Action-CoT reasoning, improving success rates and efficiency under constrained data budgets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLN methods lack explicit closed-loop mechanisms for tracking semantic progress, diagnosing execution failures, and recovering from error accumulation in long-horizon navigation, leading to brittle performance.

Method: ReflectVLN organizes decision-making through a bidirectionally interactive intention agent (subtask decomposition, reflection, corrective plans) and an execution agent (grounding subtasks into actions, monitoring progress/detecting off-track). It introduces Action Chain-of-Thought (Action-CoT), a path-conditioned dual-query training scheme for temporally coherent action generation with interpretable rationales.

Result: Experiments on standard VLN benchmarks show improved success rates and path efficiency under constrained data budgets, with lower training cost and fewer high-level intention calls at inference time, while providing interpretable intermediate decisions.

Conclusion: ReflectVLN effectively addresses error accumulation in long-horizon VLN through closed-loop bidirectional communication between agents, and Action-CoT enhances temporal coherence and interpretability of decisions.

Abstract: Existing vision-language navigation methods often couple a VLM with waypoint decoders to produce multi-step action plans, but they typically lack an explicit closed-loop mechanism for tracking semantic progress, diagnosing execution failures, and recovering from error accumulation in long-horizon navigation. To address this gap, we propose ReflectVLN, an agentic VLN framework that organizes decision-making through bidirectionally interactive intention and execution agents. The intention agent performs subtask decomposition and reflection, generating executable subtask descriptions as corrective plans. Conditioned on these descriptions, the execution agent grounds them into short-horizon actions under current observations while monitoring sub-goal progress and detecting off-track behavior. Crucially, ReflectVLN enables closed-loop bidirectional communication: the execution agent emits progress and deviation signals to trigger reflection and subtask updates on demand, and the intention agent returns structured guidance that reconditions subsequent actions for recovery. To encourage temporally coherent decisions with interpretable intermediate rationales, we introduce Action Chain-of-Thought (Action-CoT), a path-conditioned dual-query training scheme for action generation. Experiments on standard VLN benchmarks show that ReflectVLN improves success rates and path efficiency under a constrained data budget, with favorable training cost and fewer high-level intention calls at inference time, while providing interpretable intermediate decisions for analysis and collaboration. Code is available at: https://github.com/AIprogrammer/ReflectVLN

</details>


### [66] [MambaPSA: A Mamba-based Replacement for C2PSA in YOLO26](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12681)
*Sheng-Wei Chan,Chia-Min Lin,Hsin-Jui Pan,Ching-Yu Tsai,Chih-Hsiang Yang,Yung-Che Wang,Jen-Shiun Chiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes MambaPSA, a lightweight Mamba-based replacement for the C2PSA block in YOLO26, and integrates bidirectional Vision Mamba (BiViM) modules in the neck. Results show improved efficiency (2.9% fewer parameters, 12.1% fewer FLOPs, 17.6% faster CPU inference) with minimal accuracy loss, while BiViM at P4 improves mAP by 0.9.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether state space models (Mamba) can efficiently replace attention-based blocks in NMS-free object detection frameworks, achieving a better efficiency-accuracy trade-off.

Method: MambaPSA replaces the C2PSA block in YOLO26's backbone with a lightweight Mamba-based module; additionally, bidirectional Vision Mamba (BiViM) modules are inserted at P3, P4, and P5 levels of the neck.

Result: MambaPSA reduces parameters by 2.9%, FLOPs by 12.1%, and improves CPU inference throughput by 17.6% (17→20 FPS) with only -0.1 mAP50:95 change. P4 BiViM placement achieves the best accuracy gain of +0.9 mAP50:95.

Conclusion: SSMs (Mamba) provide a favorable efficiency-accuracy trade-off when replacing attention-based blocks in NMS-free lightweight object detectors.

Abstract: State space models (SSMs), notably Mamba, have recently emerged as efficient alternatives to self-attention with linear computational complexity. We investigate the integration of Mamba into YOLO26, the latest non-maximum suppression (NMS)-free object detection framework, by proposing MambaPSA, a lightweight Mamba-based replacement for the C2PSA block at the end of the backbone. To complement this study, we additionally insert a bidirectional Vision Mamba (BiViM) module at the P3, P4, and P5 levels of the neck. Experiments on PASCAL VOC 2007+2012 show that MambaPSA reduces parameters by 2.9%, FLOPs by 12.1%, and improves CPU inference throughput by 17.6% (from 17 to 20 FPS) with negligible accuracy change (-0.1 mAP50:95), while the P4 BiViM placement yields the best accuracy gain (+0.9 mAP50:95). These results suggest that SSMs offer a favorable efficiency-accuracy trade-off when replacing attention-based blocks in NMS-free lightweight detectors.

</details>


### [67] [Lesion Segmentation in Moderate to Severe Traumatic Brain Injury: An nnU-Net Based Approach with Adaptive Normalization in the AIMS-TBI 2025 Challenge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12684)
*Inhwa Son,Gaeun Lee,Sohyeon Sim,Kwang-Hyun Uhm*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents a deep learning solution using nnU-Net with adaptive intensity normalization confined to brain parenchyma for segmenting heterogeneous lesions in moderate-to-severe traumatic brain injury (msTBI) from T1-weighted MRI, achieving an Overall Dice of 0.6305 (lesion Dice: 0.4805, non-lesion Dice: 0.9324) on the AIMS-TBI 2025 Challenge test set.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Segmenting lesions in msTBI from T1-weighted MRI is clinically challenging due to high heterogeneity in lesion size, shape, and location. The AIMS-TBI 2025 Challenge was organized to stimulate development of robust segmentation algorithms.

Method: The method uses the nnU-Net framework with an adaptive intensity normalization strategy restricted to the brain parenchyma, reducing inter-subject variability and mitigating artifacts from non-brain structures.

Result: On the held-out test set, the model achieved an Overall Dice Coefficient of 0.6305, lesion Dice of 0.4805, and non-lesion Dice of 0.9324, placing competitively on the official leaderboard.

Conclusion: Incorporating anatomically constrained normalization within the nnU-Net pipeline is a powerful and effective strategy for addressing the complexities of msTBI lesion segmentation, with high specificity in distinguishing lesion from non-lesion regions.

Abstract: The segmentation of lesions in Moderate to Severe Traumatic Brain Injury (msTBI) from T1-weighted MRI presents a significant clinical challenge due to the profound heterogeneity of lesion characteristics in terms of size, shape, and location. To address this, the AIMS-TBI 2025 Challenge was organized to promote the development of robust and accurate segmentation algorithms. In this paper, we present our deep learning-based solution. Our methodology employs the nnU-Net framework with an adaptive intensity normalization strategy confined to the brain parenchyma, effectively reducing inter-subject variability and mitigating artifacts from non-brain structures. Upon final evaluation on the held-out test set, our method demonstrated highly competitive performance on the official leaderboard, achieving an Overall Dice Coefficient of 0.6305. The model obtained a Dice score of 0.4805 for lesion segmentation and 0.9324 for non-lesion tissue. While the lesion Dice reflects the difficulty of detecting highly heterogeneous lesions, the high non-lesion Dice primarily indicates the model's strong ability to correctly identify non-lesion voxels, demonstrating good specificity in differentiating lesion from non-lesion regions. These results demonstrate that incorporating anatomically constrained normalization within the nnU-Net pipeline is a powerful and effective strategy for tackling the complexities of msTBI lesion segmentation.

</details>


### [68] [Label-Decoupled Style Augmentation for Domain Generalization in Multi-Label Remote Sensing Scene Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12704)
*Alaa Almouradi,Erchan Aptoula*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a label-decoupled style augmentation framework for multi-label remote sensing domain generalization, which confines style perturbation to label-specific regions to avoid inter-class contamination, achieving 71.5% mAP (5.0 points over ERM, 1.3 over global-statistics baselines) with minimal parameter overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing feature-statistics augmentation methods (e.g., MixStyle, EFDMix) perturb channel statistics globally for each image, treating it as a single style. In multi-label classification, this allows one class to contaminate the augmentation of another. Domain generalization is understudied for multi-label remote sensing, and no prior method or multi-source benchmark exists for this task.

Method: A label-decoupled augmentation framework is proposed. Per-label attention maps (from a learnable module or gradient CAM) extract per-label feature statistics. These statistics are mixed with cross-domain samples sharing the same labels using independent per-label coefficients. Features are recomposed via attention-weighted normalization. Three operators combined with two attention sources yield six variants.

Result: Evaluated on a leave-one-domain-out benchmark from multi-label UCM, AID, and DFC15 over six shared labels. Best variant achieves 71.5% mAP, exceeding ERM by 5.0 points and the strongest global-statistics baseline by 1.3 points; largest gain on the hardest transfer is up to 7.7 points. Adds at most 0.35% parameters with unchanged inference.

Conclusion: Spatial attention and refreshed localization maps are the most influential components. The framework offers a generic, inexpensive upgrade path for multi-label statistics-based domain generalization without altering inference architecture.

Abstract: Multi-label classification assigns several co-occurring labels to each aerial scene, yet deployed models often encounter data distributions different from their training. Feature-statistics augmentation such as MixStyle, EFDMix, and correlated style uncertainty improves generalization at low cost but perturbs channel statistics globally, treating each image as a single style; one class can then contaminate the augmentation of another. Domain generalization is understudied for multi-label remote sensing; no prior method or multi-source benchmark targets it. A label-decoupled augmentation framework is therefore proposed, confining style perturbation to label-specific regions. Per-label attention, obtained from a learnable module or from gradient class-activation maps, yields per-label feature statistics; these statistics are mixed with cross-domain samples that share present labels, under independent per-label coefficients, and features are recomposed by attention-weighted normalization. Three operators combined with two attention sources produce six variants, evaluated on a leave-one-domain-out benchmark from multi-label UCM, AID, and DFC15 over six shared labels. Averaged over three splits and five seeds, the best variant attains 71.5% mean average precision, exceeding empirical risk minimization by 5.0 points and the strongest global-statistics baseline by 1.3 points, with the largest gain on the hardest transfer (up to 7.7 points). Ablations indicate that spatial attention and refreshed localization maps are most influential. The framework adds at most 0.35% parameters, leaves inference unchanged, and appears to offer a generic, inexpensive upgrade path for multi-label statistics-based domain generalization. Code is available upon acceptance at https://github.com/Alaa-Almouradi/Style-Augmentation-Upgrade.

</details>


### [69] [Color Pass-Through via Camera-Display Coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12746)
*Ruikang Li,Molin Li,Jiarui Wu,Zhe Wei,Pengpeng Liu,Tianfan Xue*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Color Pass-Through is an end-to-end learned framework that treats camera and display as a coupled system, bypassing traditional separate calibration pipelines, to better reproduce perceived scene colors on smartphone screens. It achieves +2.0 points on a 5-point user study and >2x improvement in quantitative metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The displayed image on a smartphone often differs noticeably from the original scene in color, brightness, and contrast. Traditional pipelines separate camera and display calibration and connect them via low-dimensional color transforms, causing information bottlenecks and error accumulation.

Method: Proposes Color Pass-Through, an end-to-end learned framework that operates directly on captured images by treating the camera and display as a coupled system, enabling one-step calibration for each observer via the complete capture-to-display path.

Result: Achieves an average gain of +2.0 points on a 5-point user study and more than 2x improvement on quantitative metrics compared to representative baselines, validated with both digital and human observers.

Conclusion: Coupling camera and display in an end-to-end framework significantly improves reproduction of perceived scene color, offering a systemic solution to the capture-to-display color gap.

Abstract: When a real-world scene is captured by a smartphone camera and viewed on its screen, the displayed image often differs noticeably from the original scene in color, brightness, and contrast. This gap persists despite substantial advances in both modern cameras and displays. A key reason is that most pipelines factor the high-dimensional capture-to-display process into two separately calibrated camera and display stages, and then connect them through low-dimensional color transforms, leading to information bottlenecks and inevitable error accumulation. To address this systemic challenge, we propose Color Pass-Through, an end-to-end learned framework that operates directly on captured images. Our key insight is to treat the camera and display as a coupled system rather than calibrating them in isolation. Coupling the camera and display yields two practical advantages: (1) it brings the entire real-world scenes to the display via end-to-end optimization, and (2) it allows efficient one-step calibration for each distinct observer via complete capture-to-display path. We validate Color Pass-Through using both digital and human observers. Compared with representative baselines, our method achieves an average gain of +2.0 points on a 5-point user study and more than 2x improvement on quantitative metrics, demonstrating improved reproduction of the perceived color of the original scene.

</details>


### [70] [Weakly Supervised Spatio-Temporal Candidate Discovery of Dairy Farm Sites from Seasonal Satellite Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12748)
*Usman Haider,Fatima Khalid,Karl Mason*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A weakly supervised pipeline uses seasonal Sentinel imagery and OpenStreetMap priors to rank dairy farm candidates, achieving 0.60 precision within 500m for top 5 clusters without direct farm labels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Farm labels are often incomplete, making fully supervised detection difficult. The goal is to reduce large satellite image collections into compact candidate sets for human review.

Method: Barlow Twins encoder learns multi-season tile embeddings without labels; rule-based scoring combines farm proximity, pasture evidence, and greenness; scores are smoothed via a spatial graph; tiles are grouped into ranked candidate clusters.

Result: From 26,722 tiles, 535 high-confidence tiles formed 71 candidate clusters. Top 5 clusters: 0.60 precision (500m), 0.80 precision (1000m). Top 10 clusters: 0.40 precision (500m), 0.80 precision (1000m).

Conclusion: Seasonal representation learning combined with weak geographic priors effectively generates compact, high-precision candidate sets for farm site discovery without requiring complete farm labels.

Abstract: Farm site discovery from satellite imagery is a spatiotemporal candidate ranking problem because farm evidence is distributed across pasture, field boundaries, roads, buildings, and seasonal vegetation patterns. Direct farm labels are often incomplete, which makes fully supervised detection difficult. This paper proposes a weakly supervised pipeline for ranking dairy farm candidate clusters from seasonal Sentinel imagery and open map priors. The method uses aligned spring, summer, and autumn image tiles from County Cork, Ireland, with spectral bands, vegetation indices, built area indices, and a pasture channel. A Barlow Twins encoder learns multi-season tile embeddings without farm labels. In parallel, weak OpenStreetMap farm priors are split into a prior and a held-out set. Prior features support a rule-based tile score that combines farm proximity, seasonal pasture evidence, and summer greenness, while held-out features are reserved only for proxy evaluation. The rule score is smoothed over a spatial representation graph using geographic proximity and embedding similarity, and high-scoring tiles are grouped into ranked candidate clusters. From 26,722 valid tiles, the main run selects 535 high-confidence tiles and forms 71 candidate clusters. The top 5 clusters achieve 0.60 precision within 500 m and 0.80 precision within 1000 m of held-out OpenStreetMap farm features. The top 10 clusters achieve 0.40 precision within 500 m and 0.80 precision within 1000 m. The results show that seasonal representation learning and weak geographic priors can reduce large satellite image collections into compact candidate sets for human review.

</details>


### [71] [CRC-HGD: A Histopathological Image Dataset for Grading Colorectal Cancer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12750)
*Elham Amjadi,Amin Bahreini,Sayed Mohammad Hasan Emami,Sayyed Mohammadreza Hakimian,Alireza Fahim,Hojjatollah Rahimi,Hamidreza Bolhasani*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces CRC-HGD, a histopathological image dataset of 1,914 H&E-stained colorectal adenocarcinoma images from 214 patients, graded into three WHO differentiation grades (I, II, III) across four magnification levels (4x, 10x, 20x, 40x), designed to support AI-based CRC grading research.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Colorectal cancer is a leading global cause of cancer deaths, and accurate histologic grading is critical for prognosis and treatment. To enable AI-based automated grading systems, a well-organized, multi-grade, multi-magnification labeled dataset is essential but previously lacking.

Method: Images were collected from 214 colorectal adenocarcinoma patients (Grade I: 106, Grade II: 75, Grade III: 33) using H&E-stained tissue sections acquired at Poursina Hakim Research Center (2014-2019). Grading follows WHO criteria. Each specimen is provided at four magnification levels (4x, 10x, 20x, 40x), totaling 1,914 images. The dataset is hosted on Mendeley Data and databiox.com.

Result: A publicly available histopathological dataset (CRC-HGD) containing 1,914 labeled images across all three differentiation grades and four magnifications, enabling comprehensive multi-scale computational analysis of colorectal cancer grading.

Conclusion: The CRC-HGD dataset provides a valuable resource for developing and benchmarking AI/ML/DL models for automated colorectal cancer grading, with the unique feature of labeled specimens across all three grades at multiple magnification levels.

Abstract: Colorectal cancer (CRC) is the third most common cancer worldwide and the second leading cause of cancer-related deaths globally, with approximately 1,926,425 new cases and 904,019 deaths reported in 2022. Accurate histologic grading plays a critical role in prognosis and treatment planning for colorectal adenocarcinoma. In recent years, artificial intelligence and its subcategories, including machine learning and deep learning, have been increasingly employed for automated cancer detection and classification. An appropriate and well-organized dataset is the essential first step to achieve this goal. This paper introduces CRC-HGD, a histopathological microscopy image dataset of 1,914 images obtained from 214 colorectal adenocarcinoma patients (Grade I: 106, Grade II: 75, Grade III: 33). The specimens are H&E-stained colorectal tissue sections acquired at the Poursina Hakim Research Center of Isfahan University of Medical Sciences, Iran, diagnosed between 2014 and 2019, and graded according to the World Health Organization (WHO) criteria into three grades: well-differentiated (Grade I), moderately differentiated (Grade II), and poorly differentiated (Grade III). For each specimen, four magnification levels are provided: 4x, 10x, 20x, and 40x. The dataset is accessible via Mendeley Data (https://doi.org/10.17632/yfp5sfj47m.4) and at http://databiox.com, where the latest version is also available. The distinctive feature of this dataset is the provision of labeled specimens across all three differentiation grades at multiple magnification levels, enabling comprehensive computational analysis of colorectal cancer grading.

</details>


### [72] [Hallo4D: Multi-Modal Hallucination Mitigation for Consistent Spatio-Temporal Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12752)
*Hongbo Wang,Huaibo Huang,Jie Cao,Jin Liu,Haoyang Tong,Ran He*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Hallo4D is a model-agnostic framework that mitigates spatiotemporal hallucinations (duplicated structures, jitter, identity flicker) in 3D/4D generation by using large multimodal language models (LMMs) to detect inconsistencies and guide a consensus-driven image-space optimization via multi-model voting, without retraining or architectural changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3D/4D generation methods rely on 2D diffusion supervision lacking explicit geometric consistency, causing spatial hallucinations (duplicated structures, misaligned geometry) and temporal issues (jitter, identity flicker, structural drift) in 4D. A unified, retraining-free solution for both 3D and 4D consistency is needed.

Method: Hallo4D employs a generation-detection-correction paradigm: (1) LMMs analyze multi-view/multi-frame renderings to identify spatiotemporal inconsistencies; (2) an LMM-based selector performs consensus-driven image-space optimization via multi-model voting; (3) motion-aware keyframe sampling, LMM-guided initialization, appearance alignment, exposure-aware optimization, and visibility pruning are incorporated to enhance temporal consistency, efficiency, and robustness.

Result: Hallo4D consistently outperforms strong baselines across diverse 3D and 4D generation settings, demonstrating scalable and generalizable consistency improvement.

Conclusion: The proposed paradigm provides a scalable, retraining-free, and model-agnostic solution for mitigating spatiotemporal hallucinations, improving geometric and temporal consistency in both 3D and 4D content generation.

Abstract: While recent advances in 3D generation have enabled impressive visual synthesis, existing methods often rely on 2D diffusion supervision without explicit mechanisms for geometric consistency, leading to spatial hallucinations such as duplicated structures and misaligned geometry. These issues become more severe in 4D generation, where maintaining consistency across viewpoints and temporal evolution introduces additional challenges, including jitter, identity flicker, and structural drift. We present \textbf{Hallo4D}, a unified and model-agnostic framework for mitigating spatiotemporal hallucinations in 3D and 4D content generation. Hallo4D introduces a generation-detection-correction paradigm that leverages large multimodal language models (LMMs) to identify and summarize spatial and temporal inconsistencies from multi-view and multi-frame renderings. These insights guide a consensus-driven image-space consistency optimization, where an LMM-based selector evaluates candidate corrections through multi-model voting, without requiring retraining or architectural modifications. To further improve temporal consistency and optimization efficiency, Hallo4D incorporates motion-aware keyframe sampling, LMM-guided initialization, and appearance alignment. We additionally introduce exposure-aware optimization and visibility pruning to enhance robustness under challenging viewpoints. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Hallo4D consistently outperforms strong baselines across diverse 3D and 4D generation settings, providing a scalable and generalizable solution for consistency-aware content generation.

</details>


### [73] [RFMSR: Residual Flow Matching for Image Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12753)
*Shuwei Huang,Tianyao Luo,Jicheng Liu,Daizong Liu,Pan Zhou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RFMSR proposes Residual Flow Matching for Image Super-Resolution, a vision-only framework that centers the source distribution at the LQ latent to reduce transport distance, and introduces a two-phase training strategy enabling high-quality single-step generation without sacrificing multi-step refinement, achieving SOTA perceptual quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing T2I-based ISR methods achieve high perceptual quality but suffer from massive model sizes and prohibitive training costs. Flow-matching vision-only approaches discard rich structural information in LQ inputs by transporting from pure Gaussian priors, and single-step acceleration techniques often forfeit multi-step inference capability.

Method: RFMSR is a vision-only framework that centers the source distribution at the LQ latent to reduce transport distance and preserve structural priors. It uses a two-phase training strategy: Phase I pretrains the velocity field via conditional flow matching; Phase II applies end-to-end supervision to single-step prediction while retaining velocity loss across all timesteps, enabling both high-quality single-step generation and multi-step refinement.

Result: RFMSR achieves comparable or even superior perceptual quality compared to SOTA methods, with the additional benefits of efficient single-step generation and retained multi-step refinement capability.

Conclusion: RFMSR effectively addresses limitations of existing flow-matching ISR approaches by incorporating LQ structural priors and enabling flexible inference steps, achieving competitive performance with reduced computational burden.

Abstract: Image super-resolution (ISR) has witnessed remarkable progress with diffusion models and flow matching. The dominant text-to-image (T2I) based approaches leverage large-scale foundation models as generative priors, achieving impressive perceptual quality but at the cost of massive model sizes and prohibitive training expenses. Recent flow-matching-based vision-only approaches have made significant strides; however, they adopt standard flow formulations that transport from a pure Gaussian prior to the data distribution, discarding the rich structural information already present in the low-quality (LQ) input. Furthermore, existing single-step acceleration techniques often forfeit the model's multi-step inference capability. In this paper, we propose Residual Flow Matching for Image Super-Resolution (RFMSR), a vision-only framework that centers the source distribution at the LQ latent, reducing transport distance and preserving structural priors throughout the flow trajectory. We further introduce a two-phase training strategy: Phase I pretrains the velocity field via conditional flow matching, while Phase II applies end-to-end supervision to the single-step prediction while retaining the velocity loss across all timesteps, achieving high-quality single-step generation without sacrificing multi-step refinement. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RFMSR achieves comparable or even superior perceptual quality compared to state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods. The source code is available at https://github.com/Faze-Hsw/RFMSR.

</details>


### [74] [VisCo: Leveraging Large Language Models as Intrinsic Encoders for Visual Token Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12756)
*Yupeng Zheng,Kai Zou,Bin Liu,Nenghai Yu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VisCo is a training-efficient self-compression framework that reuses a pretrained VLM as an intrinsic compressor via parameter-sharing autoencoder with memory tokens, achieving superior visual token compression across all ratios and even improving base model performance when combined with original tokens.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing visual token compression methods either rely on heuristic metrics (training-free) causing performance degradation, or introduce external compression modules that require costly retraining and compromise VLMs' prior knowledge. The key insight is that VLMs already possess strong information encoding capability, but prior approaches fail to leverage it.

Method: VisCo proposes a parameter-sharing autoencoder that reuses the pretrained VLM backbone as an intrinsic compressor. It compresses visual information through a small set of learnable memory tokens and transfers hierarchical information from the encoding phase to the decoding phase, enabling training-efficient self-compression without external modules.

Result: VisCo outperforms prior methods across all evaluated compression ratios, with larger gains under more aggressive compression. It remains stable even in the extreme single-token setting. Additionally, combining learned memory tokens with original visual tokens improves the base model, indicating complementary representation capture.

Conclusion: Reusing pretrained VLMs as intrinsic compressors is an effective and training-efficient paradigm for visual token compression. VisCo not only achieves superior compression performance but also produces representations complementary to original tokens, suggesting broader utility beyond compression.

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) process large numbers of visual tokens, resulting in substantial inference latency and memory overhead. This has motivated extensive research on visual token compression. While training-free strategies rely on heuristic metrics and suffer significant performance degradation under high compression ratios, many training-based methods introduce external compression modules that force the VLM backbone to adapt, incurring substantial retraining cost and compromising VLMs' priors. Effective visual token compression hinges on strong information encoding, a capability already present in pretrained VLMs but underutilized by existing approaches. Motivated by this, we propose VisCo, a training-efficient self-compression framework that reuses the pretrained VLM itself as an intrinsic compressor. VisCo is a parameter-sharing autoencoder that compresses visual information using a small set of memory tokens and transfers hierarchical information from encoding to decoding. Experiments show that VisCo surpasses prior methods across all evaluated compression ratios, with larger gains under more aggressive compression, and remains stable even in the extreme single-token setting. Moreover, when combined with the original visual tokens, the learned memory tokens can even improve the base model, suggesting that VisCo captures complementary representations beyond compression.

</details>


### [75] [EvoGraph-R1: Self-Evolving Multimodal Knowledge Hypergraphs for Agentic Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12764)
*Jiashi Lin,Changhong Jiang,Xiangru Lin,Ruifei Zhang,Xinyi Zhu,Jiyao Liu,Cheng Tang,Ye Du,Shujian Gao,Junzhi Ning,Lihao Liu,Ziyan Huang,Tianbin Li,Jin Ye,Junjun He*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: EvoGraph-R1 introduces a self-evolving GraphRAG framework where knowledge graphs are treated as dynamic environments shaped through agent interactions, using a Markov Decision Process for iterative retrieval, expansion, editing, and reasoning, achieving substantial improvements on multimodal VQA and text QA benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing GraphRAG methods are limited by static knowledge graphs built offline and queried in a single pass, creating three bottlenecks: text-centric fragmentation hindering cross-modal reasoning, frozen structures unable to incorporate new evidence or correct errors, and rigid single-pass retrieval without adaptive refinement.

Method: Formulates retrieval as a Markov Decision Process (MDP) where an agent observes the graph state and executes actions: GraphRetrieve (query), WebSearch (expand), GraphEdit (refine), or Answer (terminate). These actions reshape the hypergraph structure and generate feedback signals that guide subsequent evolution through a closed loop.

Result: Substantial improvements over existing RAG baselines in accuracy, coverage, and traceability on multimodal VQA and text QA benchmarks.

Conclusion: Establishes self-evolving knowledge graphs as a fundamental paradigm across modalities for knowledge-intensive reasoning.

Abstract: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) has emerged as a critical paradigm for grounding Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in external knowledge. Recent GraphRAG methods introduce structured entity-relation graphs to improve retrieval and reasoning. However, they remain limited by treating knowledge graphs as static data structures built offline and queried in a single pass. This static paradigm misaligns with the interactive, iterative nature of knowledge-intensive reasoning, creating three bottlenecks: (i) text-centric fragmentation that impedes cross-modal reasoning, (ii) frozen structures unable to incorporate new evidence or correct errors, and (iii) rigid single-pass retrieval without adaptive refinement. To overcome these limitations, we introduce EvoGraph-R1, a self-evolving GraphRAG framework that reconceptualizes knowledge graphs as dynamic environments shaped through agent interactions. We formulate retrieval as a Markov Decision Process (MDP) where the agent observes the graph state and executes actions to query (GraphRetrieve), expand (WebSearch), refine (GraphEdit), or terminate (Answer) the reasoning. These actions reshape the hypergraph structure and generate feedback signals that guide subsequent evolution. Through this closed loop, the hypergraph evolves by integrating new evidence, correcting errors, and refining structure to support multi-hop reasoning. Experiments on multimodal VQA and text QA benchmarks demonstrate substantial improvements over existing RAG baselines in accuracy, coverage, and traceability, establishing self-evolving knowledge graphs as a fundamental paradigm across modalities.

</details>


### [76] [HSEmotion Team at the 11th ABAW Challenge: Multi-Task Learning and Ambivalence/Hesitancy Video Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12774)
*Aleksei Bakin,Andrey V. Savchenko*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents winning solutions for both tasks in the 11th ABAW competition: a multi-task learning system using frozen lightweight facial extractors (MT-EmotiDDAMFN, MT-EmotiEffNet-B0) with systematic post-processing for valence/arousal/expression/AU prediction, and an audiovisual pipeline combining face, audio (HuBERT), and text (RoBERTa) classifiers with late fusion for ambivalence/hesitancy recognition, achieving strong results without fine-tuning heavy backbones.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve competitive performance in affective behavior analysis tasks without relying on heavy, fine-tuned backbone models, thereby improving efficiency and deployment flexibility compared to conventional end-to-end approaches.

Method: For multi-task learning: frozen lightweight facial extractors (MT-EmotiDDAMFN, MT-EmotiEffNet-B0) with separate heads, temporal Gaussian smoothing, per-class expression bias, AffectNet blending, per-AU threshold tuning, and weighted backbone fusion. For ambivalence recognition: late fusion of face classifier, HuBERT audio, and RoBERTa text classifiers with temporal aggregation and global-text gate.

Result: Exceeded ConvNeXt baseline significantly on multi-task validation set. Frame-level Weighted F1 improved from 0.74 (ABAW-8) to 0.79; best public-test video-level Macro F1 reached 0.73.

Conclusion: Systematic prediction calibration and lightweight multimodal fusion can rival substantially heavier end-to-end approaches while offering improved efficiency and deployment flexibility.

Abstract: This article presents our results for the 11th Affective Behavior Analysis in-the-Wild (ABAW) competition.
